### Compose native capture and graceful shutdown — 2026-10-12

The candidate `CaptureSession` now owns input, processing and synchronous sink
admission on one worker. It preserves one microphone and one sink across reads,
nonterminal flush and graceful finish. Finish completes the accepted prefix using
bounded real context from that same microphone, then closes input/DSP. If an older
flush is pending, it completes first and at most one further fence covers newer
accepted input. Immediate cancellation never drains. Sink failures, input loss,
invalid references, clock errors and timeout close the input and cannot claim
successful delivery; cancel during the sink callback prevents subsequent output.

The decision follows 72 repeatable finite-input diagnostics: padding a stopped
microphone produced substantial false residual echo, including when the reference
also stopped. A near-only impulse tail is insufficient to choose an EOS policy.
The diagnostic is recorded separately from quality gates. True finite-recording
termination remains unsupported in this candidate; no synthetic padding or fake
hardware timestamps were added to the microphone path.

### Ground availability across native lifetime — 2026-10-12

`world.ground` now distinguishes `ready`, `pending` and `unavailable`. A saved
surface awaiting startup/activation or import is not empty space. Relevant missing
ground makes `found=false`, including when a lower resident floor would otherwise
pass the support rays. Position and normal remain unavailable placeholders then.
With `ready` and `found=false`, resident geometry does not satisfy the existing
slope, step, height and footprint policy; this is not body clearance or a route.

The query uses canonical ground identities and the existing spatial index/native
residency. Conservative envelopes cover the centre and all footprint rays,
including their larger vertical band. Unknown bounds remain dependencies; known
distant ground does not block a local answer. Native envelopes combine visual and
accepted collision bounds because an uncommitted sculpt preview can be smaller
than its collider. Saved edits invalidate stale envelopes; metadata preparation
can resolve their extent without creating geometry. Ordinary props, physical-frame
surfaces, held/dynamic ground and scans remain outside authored support.

Agent inspection and user programs read the same generated fact contract. The
result reports the total dependency count and the first four stable IDs with their
states and bounds knowledge, keeping the value within the existing 1,024-character
program budget. This list is explicitly incomplete when the count exceeds four.
Reads do not activate objects, retry failed imports, start physics, change saved
revisions or write files. They use accepted colliders only when availability is
complete; envelopes never stand in for actual ground.

This is a prerequisite for regional admission, not automatic streaming. The
whole-world collision gate and 64-object/25-metre limits remain. Next work must
consume readiness in regional navigation, water and swept-physics admission,
retain dependencies while actions use them, and test cancellation and moving
boundaries before enabling automatic retirement. Device/provider holds and the
remaining acoustic/production release gates remain unchanged.

cases and skipped three private-asset cases; one new stress-test fixture exceeded
the existing four-heightfield limit. After correcting that fixture to mix four
heightfields and two imported surfaces, all seven focused cases passed, yielding
1,295 distinct verified PlayMode cases without changing production code. All
2,894 web/shared tests, lint, production build, generated source contracts, shared
boundaries and integration-driver type checks passed. This is desktop evidence;
no headset or paid provider was used.

The complete native-room integration journey and original two-page book journey
also pass using scripted responses. The new headless check reads supported and
unsupported ground through the same native transport as the book. An initial
probe point exceeded the 25-metre radial world limit; the corrected point is inside
that scope. Evidence distinguishes this fixture correction from a runtime fix.

including cold imports whose visible bounds cannot prove empty collision space.
Known distant areas remain dormant. Existing area and physical-connection
closures still determine the actual unit of activation.

The shared action lifecycle prepares those dependencies before the instant
placement effect. Native input demand now owns a residency lease through action
completion or cancellation, independently of edit/animation channel ownership.
This also closes the gap between successful loading and the next scheduler tick
for other native actions. Multiple consumers release independently. Leases reuse
the canonical retention graph and cannot survive a workspace/generation change as
current demand; they introduce no saved world copy or migration.

Placement guards are checked before loading, during preparation and again before
effect. Input edits or newly relevant destination entities invalidate an old
action; narrowing conservative bounds after loading is allowed. Human edits
remain available. Final accepted-ground, body-clearance and physical-transfer
checks still run before persistence and again after any earlier save completes.
Unavailable ground or failed imports cannot become an empty destination. Stop,
recovery, focus loss, tracking loss and preparation timeout leave placement
unchanged. The manual World origin control uses the same dependency query and
native lease; immediate resident operations still complete immediately.

Passive `world.ground` reads remain passive. Relocation does not alter terrain,
entity definitions, scene revision, motion, physics velocities or view opt-ins.
This work does not enable automatic unloading, change the whole-world physics
gate or increase the 64-object/25-metre limits. Continuous regional navigation,
water routes, swept physics, audio and larger-world performance remain required.
Device/provider/deployment holds remain in force.

### Continuous walking retains its ground and corridor — 2026-10-12

The existing world-walking control now acquires native ground and collision
candidates before each displacement. It uses the same spatial dependency query,
native preparation lane and residency ownership as shared world relocation.
A moving corridor covers the full body, every possible vertical substep, raised
clearance sweeps and support footprint. Unknown collision bounds remain
conservative dependencies. Known distant geometry stays dormant; area and
physical-connection closures still govern the actual activation unit.

Loading never queues movement. A later frame re-evaluates the current direction,
tracked foot and height; only the existing exact accepted-ground and capsule
queries can move the authored world. Pending upper ground cannot cause a drop
onto a lower resident surface. Native instances, including imported geometry,
must be active and ready before they prove clearance. The same readiness check
also strengthens explicit relocation.

A held input retains the currently needed entities through loading and use.
Changed dependency sets acquire their replacement interest before releasing the
old one. Neutral input, manipulation, focus/tracking loss, recovery, world
placement, invalid movement and workspace retirement cancel that interest.
A failed or timed-out preparation requires release of the movement stick before
retrying. Old workspace owners cannot cancel a new workspace's demand. There is
no saved motion queue, extra world-state copy or new storage migration.

Continuous dependency discovery uses current collision metadata without copying
large recipes/animation data or traversing visual renderer/skin bounds. Dormant
objects still use the existing revision-aware spatial cache, and absent or
unknown geometry never becomes proof of empty space. This removes avoidable
work; it is not a measured headset performance result.

This increment covers user-controlled walking and explicit relocation. NPC route
preparation, water-route residency, swept dynamic-body admission, audibility and
indirect observation still require matching regional contracts. The existing
whole-world physics gate, 64-object/25-metre limits and disabled automatic
unloading remain. Per-object real-room collision policies stay independent of
world presentation. Device/provider/deployment holds remain in force.

loss, invalid input and workspace unbinding. Initial fixture failures and their
corrections are retained in private evidence.

### Liquid availability across native lifetime — 2026-10-12

Finite liquids now distinguish geometric absence from unavailable native input.
`world.medium` and `object.water.traversal.path` expose the same bounded
`availability`, `dependencyCount` and `dependencies` fields to book controls,
programs and the agent. Ready, pending and unavailable describe the required
cavity placements; native collider readiness, physics admission, ownership and
each participant's real-room collision policy remain separate requirements.

Missing/inactive vessels remain candidates using their accepted cavity frame and
full dimensions, including cavities outside the object's artwork. Current native
poses and retained idle poses are projected through the shared world transform;
unknown placements remain conservative. Current live rain/pouring quantities take
precedence over an older saved fill value. Known distant and accepted-empty
cavities are excluded. The result counts every relevant missing dependency and
lists the first four stable identities, without copying recipes or animation data.

An incomplete point query reports neither containment nor active water. A nested
resident pool cannot substitute for an absent inner cavity. Upright swept water
checks refuse incomplete paths without inventing a blocking-water contact or a
water detour. Explicit ignore policy remains available for deliberate movement.
Contact sampling suppresses false crossing/exit events and re-establishes a
baseline after unavailable input; cached contact/force observations also recheck
current missing-cavity coverage. Buoyancy never applies a partial outer-water
response while relevant native input is missing. This module does not own or
freeze rigid-body gravity; dynamic-body admission remains separate work.

Reads remain passive: no loading, retry, movement, save, quantity change or
physics start. An existing owned native request, including one queued behind
another preparation group, reports pending and becomes ready after publication.
No storage migration, duplicate water simulation or extra agent-only action path
is introduced. Automatic unloading stays disabled, and the whole-world physics
gate and existing object/distance limits remain. Regional route acquisition,
water residency, swept dynamic-body admission and larger-world performance
acceptance are still required before relaxing those limits.


### Navigation owns its ground, obstacles and water — 2026-10-12

Native actor navigation now retains the required inputs of both route planning
and literal movement through the same acquisition lane as shared world actions.
A route accumulates the inputs discovered by its bounded search; each manual or
authored step replaces its local corridor. Required water uses accepted cavity
frames and dimensions, including volumes beyond their artwork. World-space
query envelopes are projected through the room frame before querying canonical
collision bounds. Known distant candidates stay out of the request; area and
physical-connection closures still determine the actual allocation unit.

Availability is checked before sampling support, querying route edges or moving.
Missing upper ground cannot be replaced by a lower resident floor. Horizontal
NavMesh snap reach and the motor's raised/substep support bands participate in
their respective queries. Pending native samples, edges and final validation
remain incomplete, never cached as an impassable edge or returned as a route.
The route is replanned after loading or accepted source changes. A failed or
timed-out load does not retry continuously; release the movement control or stop
and restart the action. Loading never queues displacement or starts physics.
Exact ground, body sweeps, water policy and environment containment still decide
every actual step.

Completed routes keep their input ownership until cancellation, a replacement
plan or movement completion. Stop, neutral manual input, arrival, authored-motion
completion, navigation disable/rebinding and workspace/physics retirement release
that demand. Separate consumers release only their own leases. The existing
shared `world.region.retention` fact reports navigation and ownership reasons;
there is no agent-only path, extra saved world or new storage format.

This supplies navigation input ownership, not finished regional simulation. The
current map still captures the bounded whole-world ground, and global physics
admission prevents ordinary walking while an authored area is dormant. The
native acquisition contract is tested independently with physics paused. Local
map construction and swept dynamic-body/support admission must replace those
whole-world assumptions before automatic unloading or larger capacities are
allowed. Per-entity real-room collision choices remain independent of visual
occlusion and passthrough opacity. Device/provider/release holds remain in force.


### Physics owns its local inputs before gravity — 2026-10-12

Rigid-body simulation now shares the native spatial-input owner used by actor
navigation. Each free solid/bouncy body retains nearby accepted ground,
collision objects and finite liquid cavities. Its envelope includes the whole
collider under rotation about the centre of mass, the bounded launch speed,
gravity and the two bounded liquid accelerations over a 200 ms lookahead, plus
accepted support columns below it. Offset water cavities use their own frames.
Unknown or disabled own collision shapes do not become empty geometry.

Admission runs before PhysX and before liquid forces. Missing upper terrain
cannot borrow a lower scan floor; missing water cannot leave gravity running
while quietly omitting buoyancy. Passive motion/contact observations also
recheck inputs. Water inspection preserves the specific missing-medium reason.
Sleeping bodies retain dependencies. Pause, grip, animation, teleport,
disable and workspace retirement release that body's ownership;
other consumers retain their own leases. During preparation only a continuously
owned body can retain transient momentum. Source changes discard held momentum;
failed preparation requires a fresh owner and cannot replay an earlier throw.

`object.physics.inputs` is a passive shared catalog fact for user forms,
programs and delegated tasks: inactive/ready/pending/unavailable, reason,
dependency count, the first four stable input IDs and transient momentum-held
state. Reading does not load, start, save or alter motion. The original book
catalog and native headless journey exercise this same fact.

This is still a prerequisite for regional simulation. Whole-world collision
admission and running-world retention remain in place. The local envelope is
not a proof for arbitrary solver impulses, tracked pushers, joint motors,
connected-body suspension or held/authored sweeps. Those consumers and regional
navigation-map construction must be covered before removing the global gate or
enabling unloading. No capacity increase or headset performance claim is made.

Continuous world-frame transfer preserves action identity and transforms retained
velocity with the authored world, including pending local preparation. Explicit
object teleport still discards previous motion. Admission is deferred across
atomic scene reconciliation, so newly created peers are all present before
readiness is judged. Fixed surfaces retain their ordinary collision events;
XRI retains held-body setup under the existing whole-world gate. An incomplete
query cannot become a settled event. Changing pending inputs does not extend
the preparation deadline, and completion after that deadline is rejected.

Focused preparation/velocity tests explicitly isolate the per-body guard from
the production whole-world admission rule. They do not establish production
regional streaming, connected-island or tracked-contact acceptance.


### Connected bodies share physics admission — 2026-10-12

Enabled, unbroken physical connections now determine a moving body's physics
input group. Area membership and remembered construction layouts do not merge
solver groups. A fixed anchor is required geometry but does not couple its
separate moving children; interrupting the anchor still invalidates each
attached group. Bodies bind explicitly to their accepted room; XRI detaching a
held object from its transform parent cannot lose that authority. Native view
retirement clears the binding. Deleted members remain missing identities, and retained broken
connections preserve their broken state when a native view retires.

The shared preparation lane acquires the union of member geometry, swept ground,
obstacles and water. Input preparation is independent of joint blocking, avoiding
the cycle where a suspended joint prevents its own required inputs from loading.
Every joint checks the group's inputs before admission, and every dynamic member
checks all required joints before the next PhysX step. Missing members, collision
shapes, environments or joints cannot leave another member running unconstrained.
Each member's real-room collision policy remains authoritative. Existing held
joints still use XRI and the production whole-world contact gate.

Only uninterrupted pending preparation may retain velocity. Grip, teleport,
animation ownership, geometry/configuration changes and member disable retire
the group's previous input owners and held momentum. Pause and workspace
retirement release all affected demand. Breaking or disabling a link separates
its physical groups; an unrelated consumer retains its own native load. A
continuous authored-world transfer keeps action identity and transforms each
member's held velocity. No incomplete group becomes a settled observation.

The existing `object.physics.inputs` catalog fact now includes connected-group
readiness without another storage format or an agent-only interface. Queries
share geometry results within a synchronous preparation/observation pass only;
fresh observations recheck geometry and owned input identities. Missing joints
and missing members cannot be repaired by reading a fact.

This does not remove whole-world collision admission or running-world retention.
Automatic unloading remains disabled and the 64-object/25-metre limits remain.
Tracked/kinematic contact sweeps, arbitrary solver impulses and joint-motor
bounds, regional navigation construction, larger-world budgets and actual Quest
performance/immersion acceptance are still required before regional streaming.
Pending-load fixtures explicitly isolate local admission from the production
global gate; they are not proof that full regional streaming is ready.

### Tracked contact and manual-grab preparation — 2026-10-13

Hand/controller push volumes and physical XRI grabs now acquire their latest
swept ground, obstacle and water inputs through the same owned native-preparation
lane as navigation and connected bodies. No displacement is queued during a
load. A waiting grab keeps its current pose; a waiting push volume is non-solid
and follows the current tracked source. Rotation, offset colliders and two-hand
resizing contribute to the conservative grab envelope. The final grab transformer
runs after XRI's pose calculation and before its target and throw sampling.

Pause, loss of tracking/selection, workspace replacement, geometry changes and
animation takeover release the relevant owner. Restart needs fresh input; a
previous throw history must age out before a release can throw again. XRI removes
its final selector before calling public release listeners, so that final
readiness read has a synchronous release scope before retiring the lease.
Tracked jumps and tracking gaps disable the push volume; re-entry inside a
creation stays non-solid until the hand moves clear. Held-object collider
exclusions are restored after re-enabling the contact. None of this delays the
rendered hand/controller pose or adds a flat UI outside the book.

The generated catalog adds passive `object.contact.inputs` and
`controller.contact.inputs` facts, shared by the book forms, agent and headless
client. They expose inactive/ready/pending/unavailable, a bounded reason and
full dependency count plus the first four IDs. Reading never starts a load,
physics or an edit. The object fact covers physical human grabs of creations;
scripted attachments remain a separate existing motion owner. Native publication
refreshes a snapshot of its objects because preparing a released body can
synchronously activate another required object.

The per-entity environment policy still decides whether scanned floors and walls
apply. Visual occlusion, passthrough opacity and physical room collision remain
separate decisions. These local sweeps do not remove whole-world collision
admission or running-world retention. Authored/attached kinematic motion,
arbitrary solver impulses and joint-motor bounds, regional navigation, larger
budgets and actual Quest performance still need acceptance before enabling
regional unloading. Automatic unloading remains off; the 64-object/25-metre
scope is unchanged.

### Carried-prop input preparation and playback timing — 2026-10-13

Carried creations use the same owned ground/obstacle/water input lane as manual
contacts. The current prop-to-anchor swept collider envelope is prepared before
following the anchor. While pending, the prop stays at its accepted pose and
its playback lifetime freezes; old throw samples are discarded. Resume checks
the latest anchor and the existing real native clearance checks, without queuing
missed motion. Throwing immediately after a wait requires enough new motion;
loading must never replay momentum from before the wait. Drawing/sculpting tips
on a waiting attachment do not continue editing a surface. Accepted edits to
already loaded dependencies retire the stale input lease and recheck the latest
geometry, without resetting the wait budget or reusing old throw samples. Failed
or cancelled loads still stop the action.

A fitted full-body animation and its prop share a bounded playback clock. The
scheduler consumes native remaining playback time instead of restarting the
entire duration or falsely completing during a later wait. Imported avatar
clips sample that clock, recorded motion uses it, and an owned Animator freezes
while waiting. A standalone `object.hold` owns only its prop and cannot stop an
independently animated holder. Multiple preparation consumers share a cumulative
30-second wait budget; cancellation retires the exact inputs and restores the
owning avatar's normal playback. Ordinary timed actions keep their existing
scheduler behavior.

`object.attachment.inputs` is a passive typed catalog fact for these attachments,
shared by the book, agent and headless clients. It exposes the same bounded
native-input status as manual contact facts, without moving the prop or loading
anything. Receipt outcomes still distinguish actual release from planned motion.
Per-entity real-room collision policies remain authoritative; visual occlusion
and reality-layer opacity are separate from physical contact.

This increment does not prove future recorded-root or deforming-limb envelopes,
arbitrary solver impulses, joint motors, regional navigation or large-world
performance. Root-motion input admission is the next separate consumer. The
whole-world gate and running-world retention remain, automatic unloading stays
off, and the current 64-object/25-metre scope remains. Headset and paid-provider
acceptance remain on hold.

### Recorded-root input preparation — 2026-10-13

Recorded program playback, recorded throws and the manual animation preview
share one native-input owner. It prepares the complete bounded recorded root
path before applying a pose: intermediate keys and loop boundaries are included,
and the pivot envelope covers every rotation and the largest recorded uniform
scale. This deliberately loads the bounded recording's input region before
playback rather than treating an unrendered key as empty space. It is not a
second copy of the saved world or an automatic unloading policy.

Playback time pauses while required geometry loads. A fitted prop and recorded
root use the same cumulative wait budget, and completion cannot bypass pending
root inputs. Manual preview keeps its Play state while preparing and releases
its exact demand on Stop. A changed room frame, target/ownership loss, failed
load or expired wait ends playback. Existing water-policy checks run only after
inputs are ready. Accepted dependency edits recheck the recording's current
world envelope.

The passive `object.recording.inputs` fact exposes the same bounded status to
users, agents and headless clients. Observations do not acquire geometry or
advance playback. Authored imported-clip travel, deforming-limb envelopes,
solid-path validation for arbitrary recorded poses, skipped-key water traversal
and general solver/motor envelopes remain separate work. Complete input
availability must not be confused with proof that the path is collision-free.
The production whole-world collision gate and running retention still apply;
automatic unloading and larger-world budgets remain disabled.

### Intermediate authored-motion body paths — 2026-10-13

Embedded and reusable motions now carry a derived timeline from validated source
bytes. It retains every source key time and cubic component turning point, with
120 Hz substeps between them. Loop seams retain both source poses, reverse
sampling traverses the same interval backwards, and fractional loop timestamps
finish on the exact modulo pose requested by playback. This metadata is derived
when loading existing files; it introduces no saved format or motion-ID change.

Authored travel samples the bounded interval before moving Maestro. Its combined
body and ground envelope prepares intermediate obstacles and water through the
existing owned input lane. Once ready, every sampled segment must pass supported
ground, viewer-distance, body clearance and the actor's water policy. Only then
is the final imported pose and placement committed. A failed segment restores the
last accepted full pose; a waiting interval retains its original requested time.
Per-entity real-room participation stays authoritative and independent of visual
occlusion, passthrough and sound.

At most 256 samples are permitted for one interval. An oversized seek or catch-up
fails without publishing a partial pose or silently dropping source keys. This
bounds sample/query count, not measured headset frame time. It is a sampled body
path, not a proof of a fully continuous curve between samples or swept deforming
limbs. Parent-rotation curves, full limb/prop contacts, regional admission,
automatic unloading and larger-world budgets still need their own evidence.
The production whole-world collision gate and current world-size limits remain.

obstacles, exact library motion identity, reverse traversal, cubic excursions,
water avoid/ignore policies, complete pose retention and excessive-seek refusal.

### Native solver sweep evidence — 2026-10-13

Eleven new native stress cases now compare the actual post-step collider bounds
with the pre-step input envelope. Ten isolate PhysX with production bodies,
joints, contact inputs and liquid forces. Another observes the normal automatic
FixedUpdate/physics loop without replacing input preparation. All 69 related
physics/contact/connection/medium regressions passed, with no skips. The test
source and verification mirror match; production runtime and catalog remain
identical to `652bd77f`.

At the tested 0.02-second physics step on Windows Unity 6000.3.24f1:

| Native case | Peak speed | Largest measured step |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Opposed bouncy impact, mass ratio 400:1 | 36.44 m/s | 0.451 m |
| Maximum spring/force slider at scale 4 | 55.00 m/s | 1.100 m |
| Continuous tracked push, 0.49 m per tick | 26.95 m/s | 0.442 m |
| Fixed chain with mass ratio 400:1 and spin | 23.70 m/s | 0.646 m |

The chain reached 111.12 rad/s, and a breaking joint reached 38.55 rad/s, despite
the configured 30 rad/s maximum. All measured collider bounds remained inside
the existing preparation envelope; the smallest measured margin was 4.768 m.
The automatic-update impact reproduced the 36.44 m/s observation. Other cases
cover overlap recovery, sleeping-body wakeup, offset rotating colliders, a hinge
motor and real buoyancy/drag. This validates these scenarios, not arbitrary solver
conditions, intermediate continuous paths, larger time steps or headset cost.

The implementation decision is explicit: do not replace the whole-world gate
with a formula that assumes 15 m/s or 30 rad/s are post-solver bounds. Unity's
[linear-velocity documentation](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.3/Documentation/ScriptReference/Rigidbody-maxLinearVelocity.html)
and these native observations contradict that assumption. The next regional
admission work must preserve collision coverage even when native solver output
exceeds the prediction. Investigate conservative dormant collision proxies from
accepted spatial metadata, with owned loading and suppressed placeholder contact
events; unknown geometry must remain unavailable. A proxy must not be reported
as a real surface, ground height or completed interaction. Keep the production
gate until that safeguard and ownership transitions have native evidence.

No physics limits, unloading behavior or simulation semantics changed in this
increment. This test-only work does not require repeating the unchanged web/book
journeys; their complete verification remains recorded above for `652bd77f`.
Receipts are under `.quest-evidence/solver-sweeps`. Device, paid-provider,

### Dormant collision guards — 2026-10-13

Retiring native objects now keeps a conservative box around their last accepted
collision extent. The guard is published before the real collider is detached,
follows accepted placement and world-frame changes, and remains through cancelled
or failed activation. Successful activation replaces it without a frame gap.
Deletion, workspace replacement and owner destruction disable it synchronously.
These guards are runtime resources; they do not change saved objects or revisions.

Unknown collision and known-empty collision remain different. A geometry edit
disables the stale guard. Preparing visual metadata does not establish physical
collision: a fresh guard requires accepted collision bounds from rebuilt native
geometry. This preserves the existing unknown-input refusal rather than using an
object's appearance as proof of a usable floor or obstacle.

A dedicated native layer lets guards stop rigid items while excluding controller
contact, pointing, drawing, sculpt packing, liquid queries, ground/navigation,
weather cover and world-movement geometry. Development ray diagnostics now use
the same visible-hit path as normal book input. Guard impacts never emit authored
object collision events; the temporary box has no renderer, selectable identity,
ground provider or acoustic representation.

`DormantCollisionTests.cs` covers lifecycle replacement, cancellation, placement,
frame transforms, geometry invalidation, deletion/Undo, workspace replacement,
known-empty/unknown extents, query filtering and a real native rigid-body impact.
The production whole-world physics admission gate remains covered and unchanged.

This establishes collision coverage, not completed regional simulation. The
following owned-interruption increment adds contact-triggered preparation and an
explicit restart from rest. Seamless collision handoff, post-step motion
guarantees and regional navigation still need native evidence before automatic
unloading or the whole-world restriction can change.
The existing 64-object/25-metre limits and all device/provider/release holds remain.

`c297fef8a99d41649203e6e3b6cf7895` passed. Both clients and editors

fixtures are refreshed from these runs; verification receipts are under
`.quest-evidence/dormant-collision`.


### Owned interruption after a dormant collision — 2026-10-13

A guard contact now queues the body, target, collision generation, placement,
world frame and pre-step physical group. The PhysX callback does not load an
object or mutate a body. The editor drains that queue before the next physics
step, registers the complete connected-group hold, and acquires the exact native
inputs through the existing independently owned demand system. Separate moving
children of a fixed anchor remain independent; several contacts in one connected
group share its hold and prepare every contacted target.

The completed solver pose is retained, but velocity is discarded. Loading exact
geometry does not replay an old throw or silently continue an uncertain impact.
The group stays stopped until an explicit physics Pause then Start, or a grab,
resumes it from rest. `object.physics.inputs.interrupted` and its reason expose
the same state to the book, agent and headless clients. Passive reads cannot load
geometry. Unrelated physical groups continue normally in the isolated native
regressions; production still requires the whole-world collision gate.

Pause, grip, animation ownership, teleport, geometry changes, deletion and
workspace retirement invalidate the appropriate recovery owner. Already completed
loads may remain resident; cancellation is not an instruction to delete reusable
native geometry. Other consumers retain their own preparation. A source or frame
change during recovery keeps an explicit interruption rather than claiming stale
readiness, and a ready lease continues to recheck its accepted inputs.

This does not make a coarse box a faithful surface. Direct guard impacts are not
authored collision events, but joint breaks or secondary contacts caused by that
solver step still need an explicit policy and native evidence. CCD/tunneling,
rapid rotation, seamless collider replacement, regional navigation and the cost
of guards at large scale remain open. Keep whole-world admission, running-world
retention, disabled automatic unloading and the 64-object/25-metre limits until
the end-to-end replacement is proven. Device/provider/release holds are unchanged.

Native Headless journey `c0fd04eb567a4b15a4c46e5117c0dc6c` passed with

### Completed-step provenance for interrupted physics — 2026-10-13

The editor records dynamic state and physical-group membership after fixed-step
prop/catch ownership changes, before PhysX. Joint-break callbacks may release
normal preparation leases without losing that record. Dynamic contact edges from
Enter/Stay callbacks extend the affected group; fixed floors and anchors do not
couple otherwise independent moving bodies. Recovery retains the completed pose,
discards momentum and keeps explicit from-rest restart semantics.

Native collision and connection-break events now carry `interrupted`. Original
listeners and payloads are captured during the callback in the existing bounded
scheduler queue. Delivery waits for completed-step classification. It marks any
participant connected to a guard-affected moving group, without claiming the guard
caused a particular collision or joint break. Genuine contacts remain observable,
actual broken joints remain latched, and direct guard impacts remain excluded.
Before classification, Pause/Start intent changes and workspace/lifecycle
cancellation discard unfinished observations. Classified observations follow
ordinary captured-listener delivery; later pauses do not rewrite completed
collisions. An old observation cannot reach a later program wait. The book's
block editor and agent share the generated event definition.

This closes the prior callback-order/provenance gap for covered native scenarios.
It does not enable regional physics, seamless solver handoff or automatic
unloading. CCD/tunneling, persistent guard contacts across ownership transitions,
failed imports during long recovery, broad motor/rotation behavior, regional
navigation, large-world cost and device acceptance remain separate gates. Keep
whole-world admission, running retention and the 64-object/25-metre limits.

### Contact continuity and independent recovery ownership — 2026-10-13

Continuing native guard contact now requeues through OnCollisionStay. A body
already touching a guard can request recovery after its prior observation was
cancelled; a fresh collision Enter is not required. Guard callbacks still copy
observations only and do not create authored collision events.

Grabbing, teleporting or taking animation control cancels recovery for the
changed actor's current physical constraint island. It does not erase the
completed native step or resume other free bodies that merely collided with
that island. Remaining actors retain their hold and independently owned native
preparation. Pre-step body identity and placement reject replaced/teleported
owners. Existing fixed-anchor separation and explicit from-rest restart remain.

An active catch now reports the real motion-unavailable reason instead of always
blaming user/action ownership. Its shared fact sanitizes and bounds display text
to the existing program-value limit. This avoids turning a legitimate recovery
reason into a failed fact read. The catch result still requires actual native
capture, and interrupted preparation is not settlement.

Seven new tests distinguish controlled contact/ownership ordering from actual
native contact. XRI grips before and after drain, teleport, and connected animation
takeover leave an incidental free neighbour interrupted and preparing. A native
sphere pressed into a guard produces Enter then Stay without another Enter.
That isolated fixture bypasses ordinary preparation between manual steps; it is
not complete production ownership-handoff evidence. The catch pair uses the same
actual secondary-body contact with/without a distant guard: the interrupted body
has zero catches and zero settlements; the uninterrupted control is caught.
The extended manual collision step is explicitly drained before returning to
ordinary catch FixedUpdate timing. Passive book/headless observations remain
separate from active native collision evidence.

Whole-world physics admission, running-world retention, automatic unloading off,
and the 64-object/25-metre scope remain. Broader grabbed/motor/rotation transitions,
failed-import recovery, CCD/tunneling, regional navigation, larger-world cost and
headset acceptance remain open. These tests do not establish seamless streaming.

objects remain intact and neither file repair nor an independent owned load

spin. An incidental contact participant keeps its hold after the other is grabbed;
unrelated bodies continue running.

### Grounded shallow-water exits — 2026-10-13

An actor whose policy avoids water can now leave an already-wet shallow position
by approaching a nearest cavity edge or shallower ground. The whole segment must
remain within its saved maximum depth and the body-height cap, and cannot deepen
beyond the body-scaled numerical tolerance. Dry entry, crossing toward a farther
shore, entering another pool and unavailable liquid inputs still refuse movement.
Ground and solid clearance remain separate required checks for walking.

This extends the existing per-actor policy and the same live liquid geometry used
by walking, recorded root movement and passive path facts. There is no additional
agent-only command or saved format. The catalog explains that a permitted segment
does not prove the actor has reached dry ground. A zero maximum depth disables
meaningful shallow egress. Swimming, automatic escape-route planning, arbitrary
NPC movement and terrain reservoirs remain open.

passed **13 EditMode / 43 PlayMode** checks. New coverage includes rectangular and

### Terrain-backed measured water — 2026-10-13

A fixed object with one canonical height field can now use that same surface as
its reservoir bed through `object.container.edit` with `terrainFloor=true`.
Frame, footprint and capacity derive from the terrain; liquid amount remains
explicit saved state. Gravity-relative level, clipped shoreline rendering,
medium sampling, swept body checks, cup filling, pouring, rain, buoyancy and
contact/ripple events share the accepted geometry and quantity. Sculpting updates
capacity while preserving water; an overflow or failed save refuses before
publication. Undo and reusable prototypes preserve the same contract.

Room format is 37, terrain-container component is v3, and prototypes carrying it
use v6. Existing non-terrain prototypes remain accepted. Missing native terrain
reports unavailable rather than inventing a rectangular bed. Reservoirs are
bounded equalized cavities with an explicit perimeter/opening; those boundaries
do not create solid walls. Separate basins need separate owners. River flow,
low-bank spill propagation, displacement, swimming and aquatic NPCs remain open.
Follow planning conservatively uses the outer footprint, while actual segments
check wet terrain triangles. Dense rain-level updates still need Quest profiling.

### Longer WAV recordings share bounded playback — 2026-10-13

Accepted WAV sources now support 0.03–600 seconds within the existing 32 MiB
file and 32-file/128 MiB private library bounds. Short clips remain resident;
clips over thirty seconds keep an exact hash-verified file and a shared cache of
at most sixteen one-second PCM blocks. The same source definition, emitter,
voice owner, controls, event programs, imports, chat transfer and Undo apply.
Tones retain their thirty-second limit; saved identities and formats are unchanged.

The global two-worker lane handles file reads and resampling. Every playback has
its own consumed position, pause state and output ring while sharing source
blocks. Missing blocks report preparation, never completion. Only native PCM
consumption and the output tail complete a finite play. Stop cancels the last
source owner and retains its reservation until pending work and the file close.
Immutable reader retention allows archive reads and makes workspace retirement
wait for file closure without counting a playing recording as an active write.
Existing freeze policies still apply. Eight-source/eight-voice admission and the
process-wide PCM ceiling remain; each long-source cache reserves 768,000 bytes.
These counters exclude temporary source reads, per-voice rings and downstream DSP.

The common catalog exposes matching duration and receipt limits. `audio.start`
starts background music independently of its initiating task; `audio.play` waits
for the finite sound. Imports never implicitly play. Other codecs, live sources,
echo/capture coexistence and physical acoustic acceptance remain separate work.

Verification: **1,193 EditMode / 1,461 PlayMode passed**, with three expected
optional private-asset skips. New coverage compares block/resident resampling,
bounded file reads and cache eviction, independent playback/pause/resume/stop,
waiting-worker cancellation, damaged files, archive reads and workspace retirement.
The native player also consumed the complete thirty-two-second recording.

## Shared grounded following — 2026-10-13

Created actors and Maestro now share the timed `object.follow` action and passive
`object.navigation` fact. Following delegates every accepted step to the same
engine as finite `object.navigate`; it does not add another planner, scheduler or
saved behavior format. The leader remains independently controllable. Per-object
real-room/water policy, accepted ground, native preparation and lifecycle rules
still apply. Detailed semantics and limitations are in
[world authoring](QUEST_WORLD_AUTHORING.md#shared-grounded-following--2026-10-13).

being passed to the regex engine. The validator now treats it as absent while
retaining length, control-character and actual-pattern checks.

## Shared object lamps and lighting controls — 2026-10-14

Point/spot lamps now belong to created or imported objects and named recipe parts.
The catalog exposes saved editing plus transient enabled/colour/intensity controls,
with passive list/detail facts. The original book generates the same controls that
agents and programs use. Runtime overrides have exact state guards, no room writes
or Undo entries, and survive renderer recreation. Source edits and workspace
boundaries restore defaults; copies and prototypes preserve only saved definitions.
Room format 39 and prototype v8 retain exact lamp definitions. The complete contract
is in [world authoring](QUEST_WORLD_AUTHORING.md#object-lamps-and-transient-controls--2026-10-14).

The shared watercolor shader supports additive local illumination and optional hard
virtual shadows. Cutouts preserve their holes; blended/faded surfaces transmit
light. Readable book pages and control text remain independent. This does not add
sun shadows, coloured glass transmission, relighting of camera pixels or shadows
from real-room obstacles. Limits are two lamps per object, four per room and six
reserved shadow-map faces, including disabled definitions. These are admission
bounds, not measured Quest performance guarantees.

## Off-screen lighting dependencies — 2026-10-14

An off-screen lamp or shadow caster can affect a visible scene. The shared native
retention graph now includes a `lighting` reason for effective lamps and potential
casters in their conservative influence bounds. Viewer prediction, grace periods,
scoped cameras, dormant display preparation and virtual captures use the same
calculation. Disabling a lamp or setting zero intensity releases its current
lighting demand. Influence bounds remain separate from occupied geometry.

Recipe attachments account for ancestor rotation without copying recipes every
frame. Exact scanned-plane bindings reproject through the common room frame;
intentionally hidden unmatched layers do not block capture. Captures prepare
required inputs and wait for pending imported shadow geometry. Missing assets or
unavailable native lamps fail explicitly and preserve the previous published
snapshot. Source formats remain room 39 / prototype 8, with no migration.

## Asynchronous walking-map construction — 2026-10-14

Walking maps now use Unity's asynchronous native builder. Each operation owns
immutable mesh copies until completion or cancellation drains, with at most two
native jobs admitted at once. Changed ground retires the old installed map before
another step; only a result matching current geometry can publish. A completed
map uses the current physical frame. Pausing, disabling, rebinding or destroying
its owner cannot let a late completion install into a replacement room.

Current colliders still support bounded direct manual/exact steps while planned
walking and following wait. Authored animation preserves the first pending sample
time, pauses the existing clock and restores its accepted pose. When ready, the
whole sampled interval still passes the same support, user-clearance, solid and
water checks before any pose commits. The shared authored-input fact preserves
its dependency detail while reporting a pending map. There is no new action,
planner, room/prototype format or migration.

The complete final native suite passed **1,205 EditMode / 1,536 PlayMode** checks,
with the three expected optional private-asset skips. Eight new cases cover
current-ground traversal, geometry replacement, canceled publication, frame
relocation, bounded concurrent work, retiring stale routes, pending authored time
and destruction cleanup. Existing navigation tests now wait for actual map
completion while retaining collision, route and ownership assertions.

Complete offline Headless **6eb952b923b24fd68862f4e94b723abc** and Original Book
**ab7f5c09e7584bea85e00958938d38f0** journeys passed with zero client/editor exits.
Both execute actual native movement using an explicitly selected synthetic gait,
and report completed travel of approximately two metres through the shared action
path. They use synthetic assets/scripted providers; they do not establish provider
or physical-headset acceptance.

## Shared MP3 and Vorbis imports — 2026-10-14

Native files and finished local chat attachments now accept Layer III MP3 and
single-stream Ogg Vorbis through the existing sound preview/acceptance owner.
Original chat bytes must match their selected hash before conversion. Compressed
inputs normalize to 24 kHz mono 16-bit WAV; acceptance uses the new preview hash.
Existing WAV bytes, saved source/emitter/program definitions, private libraries,
archives, Undo and bounded short/long playback retain their current behavior.
There is no additional provider client, live-stream adapter or automatic playback.

Pinned managed NLayer 3.0.0 and NVorbis 0.10.5 source builds under Mono and IL2CPP.
Complete frame/container preflight, bounded Vorbis setup tables, cancellation,
a two-minute deadline and the shared two-worker lane constrain import work.
Limits remain 32 MiB, mono/stereo, 8–96 kHz and 0.03–600 seconds. Opus, chained Ogg,
free-bitrate MP3 and compressed WAV remain unsupported. Source pins, original and
shipped hashes, explicit local patches, synthetic fixture provenance and MIT/BSD
notices are recorded; web CI checks their integrity. FFmpeg only authored fixtures
and independent reference PCM and is not an application dependency.

Independent MP3 reference comparisons caught startup synthesis delay and an 8 kHz
short-block decoding error after the initial duration checks passed. Both were
fixed before final verification. The earlier full run was interrupted to avoid
accepting superseded source. Regression checks compare complete PCM duration,
root-mean-square error and the first/last 20 milliseconds against the independent
reference output. Lossy-codec timing still depends on encoder metadata; exact
sample-aligned source material should use WAV.

## Shared world audio levels — 2026-10-14

`audio.mix` and `audio.mix.control` give the user, agent and event-driven programs
one guarded interface for master/effects/media/ambience levels. The generated book
form loads the same exact runtime state ID used by native commands. Levels apply
to current, preparing, paused and future world sounds, multiplying each instance's
own gain without restarting it, altering its control revision or rewriting its
saved emitter. Zero gain continues normal consumption and resource accounting.

This is transient workspace state with no room-format migration, save or Undo.
Temporary Begin/Discard, reload/workspace replacement and editor disable reset it;
source/emitter edits and renderer recreation preserve it. Independent speech/web
players and capture policy remain unchanged. Reflection/AEC quality failures and
continuous-background-audio/Live acceptance remain unresolved. No automatic
unloading, world-limit expansion or physical/paid-provider claim is added.

### Shared Maestro attention — 2026-10-14

The native `avatar.look` action directs the existing head-gaze layer at the
tracked user, one exact object or an authored room point. Its schema generates
the original book form; agents and programs invoke the same module. It owns only
Maestro's gaze channel and observes the focus object without claiming its motion.
Native dependencies use the existing acquisition lifecycle. Missing/replaced
targets, unavailable bounds, editing, tracking loss and normal room/model
boundaries stop the action without a restart. Upper-body gestures remain
independent; existing walking/whole-body ownership conflicts are preserved.

`avatar.attention` exposes the last sampled focus, authored point and head
offsets, including range limits and reduced-motion suppression. The completion
receipt preserves its last sample; passive observation is neutral after Stop.
This reuses the selected Maestro avatar's canonical/retargeted pose path and
adds no provider, saved pose, migration or second character controller.
Arbitrary created NPC gaze, eye/visibility recognition and physical acceptance
remain separate work. See [the user/agent contract](QUEST_WORLD_AUTHORING.md#directing-maestros-attention).

enlarge world limits, enable per-area physics or remove whole-world collision

### Physics dependency retention during area retirement — 2026-10-14

Verified on desktop. Automatic retirement now checks the existing
per-body and physical-island swept collision, ground and water dependencies.
It retains active, sleeping and pending dynamic identities and the complete
connected dependency set. Unknown actor sweeps retain the environment instead
of proving a distant area unused. Dormant areas no longer independently trigger
the global collision stop; existing per-consumer preparation and conservative
barriers still apply. A ready body preserves motion when an unrelated static
area retires or publishes. Interrupted-contact status keeps the stopped state,
restart-from-rest instruction and primary cause visible in shared short facts.

Scope remains deliberate: explicit shared Start acquires all saved participants;
resident imported-geometry failures still stop globally; leaving an accepted
simulation boundary still has the current pause policy. Selective startup,
local failure/boundary handling, hardware performance and increased capacity
remain outstanding. Per-entity real-room participation remains independent of
visual layers, opacity, virtual terrain and these residency decisions.

### Canonical cleanup for unavailable objects — 2026-10-15

The ordinary object.delete action now removes an exact saved creation without
requiring or loading its native instance. The book form, agent and programs use
the existing capability, shared owner checks and persisted journal transaction.
Dormant objects and creations whose native root disappeared can be removed.
The included book and Maestro remain protected. Held targets, animation and
authoring ownership, native preparation/retirement, material transfers and
workspace write/read-only restrictions still apply.

Only objects changed by an accepted edit can recreate missing native views.
Cleanup, Undo and Redo leave unrelated missing views unavailable. Missing
imported geometry stays registered with collision admission, so editing a peer
cannot silently turn unknown geometry into empty space. Cleanup never starts
physics. Saved connection references remain intact and dependent actors report
missing inputs until explicitly repaired or restored by Undo.

A failed save preserves source, revisions and history. Undo restores exact
saved identity, model/animation data and area membership through ordinary native
preparation; it does not promise unavailable assets will load. Receipt replay
does not repeat deletion after Undo. No new agent API, storage migration or
capability argument schema was introduced.

## Simulation intent and per-actor readiness — 2026-10-15

The world now distinguishes explicit enablement from available ground. Start can
complete with `running=true, ready=false`: enabled, waiting for accepted ground.
`canStart` reports world collision/lifecycle admission, not actual movement
readiness. Every body still needs its selected ground column and owned native
inputs before gravity, impulses or walking. The shared action, facts and tool
controls use this same runtime; no new storage, capability ID or schema is added.

This supersedes the tracked-view Start fallback from the preceding checkpoint.
An empty or fixed-only world does not load ground at the viewer just to enable
physics. A virtual character can prepare its own local ground for an accepted
walk even with global real-room collisions enabled and no aligned scan. Loading
or retiring native ground changes readiness, not the user's simulation intent.
Explicit Pause, focus/lifecycle holds, scan changes and collision-policy changes
retain their existing invalidation rules. Required free-body preparation can
still fail; enabled does not promise a clear path or available assets.

A previously simulating actor whose selected ground disappears is held with its
affected physical group; independent bodies continue. Repair does not replay its
old throw or silently resume it. Grab or Pause/Start creates a fresh owner from
rest. An actor that has never had ground simply waits. Object input observations
now require its ground admission as well as prepared geometry, so they cannot
report ready while the body is waiting outside accepted ground. Specific recovery
errors (changed inputs, missing/damaged imports and world movement) remain visible
ahead of generic waiting/interruption guidance. Missing support and interruption
are not settlement events.

Moving the world during initial walking preparation cancels that request and its
pending inputs. Idle navigation maps also release invalid ground without waiting
for another route query. Larger loading groups, conservative unknown-geometry
isolation, readable readiness summaries, continuous-follow idle demand and
country-scale capacity still need work. Hardware acceptance remains outstanding.

empty-world waiting, scan-free virtual walking, stale requests, Pause and
world-frame cancellation.

map still checks current navigation geometry every update. Moving away resumes normal owned

and explicit preference changes retain their existing full cleanup. Repair or
resuming physics never restarts an interrupted follow command.

## Independent home loading — 2026-10-15

Unassigned home creations previously had implicit edges to every other home
creation. Viewing one therefore kept unrelated distant objects resident. A new
native regression reproduced this before runtime changes. Named authored regions
and enabled physical connections remain atomic; the implicit home edges are gone.

Automatic retirement now chooses a deterministic dependency group. Explicit
whole-region and automatic target operations share one prepared native adoption
path, which checks the exact closure, poses, live ownership, tracking admission,
source revisions and acoustic reservations before detaching any view. Whole-home
operations additionally guard their member list across asynchronous preparation.
Failure state belongs to the failed group and is aggregated into the existing
shared region report, so failure does not prevent unrelated home retirement.

Inspection and selection also use saved identity. The offline Headless journey
exposed a refusal after a newly created cup retired; inspection had incorrectly
required a live view. Saved recipes now remain inspectable without loading them.
The saved physics-settings fact also no longer depends on a native instance.
Saved imported-character gait choices remain readable while their native model
is unloaded or preparing; availability and owned playback stay separate.
Missing parts and deleted objects still fail, and live actions retain their
existing native-presence requirements.

The change preserves saved state, IDs, versions, schemas and current budgets.
Partial residency is reported by existing resident/retained counts, not a second
user or agent scene. Named-area granularity, unknown-geometry isolation and
larger capacity remain separate work. No headset performance claim is made.

## Immutable retention topology — 2026-10-15

Routine journal queries previously rebuilt dependency edges and region lists even
when saved connections were unchanged. The journal now lazily builds immutable
connected groups. Loading and input guards query that topology directly; live
retention reports allocate fresh demand flags for those groups. No live native
object, ownership decision, recipe or mutable saved array is cached in topology.

Only accepted identity/dependency edits invalidate the cache. History and snapshot
publication share that invalidation. Pose, paint, joint settings and region names
can preserve it. Forks share immutable data while preserving independent saved
state and live demand; retained observations keep their original dependency view.

The initial .NET allocation-counter attempt returned zero on this runtime and was
rejected as unsupported evidence. Unity's GC.Alloc recorder reproduced 13,100
allocation events for 100 warm journal queries at 64 creations before the change.
This is an allocation-event count, not byte usage or a headset frame-time result. The regression also requires a working recorder.
The measurement uses Unity's documented current-thread recording mode:
[ProfilerRecorder options](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/Unity.Profiling.ProfilerRecorderOptions.CollectOnlyOnCurrentThread.html).

Verified on desktop: **1,270 EditMode / 1,648 PlayMode** passed, with three
expected optional private-asset skips. **41 focused EditMode / 51 PlayMode** checks
cover immutable readback, fresh reasons, connection edits, deletion/recreation,
regions, no-op edits, prepared acceptance/cancellation, forks, snapshots and history,
alongside existing native acquisition, startup and retirement cases.

Unity's current-thread GC.Alloc recorder measured **13,100 → 900
allocation events** for 100 warm journal read/retain/closure queries at 64 creations.
Reconstruction with the new topology measured 93,900 events. Cold builds
allocate more because connected groups are precomputed; the benefit applies to
reused queries, not every edit. The pre-change regression failed; the post-change bound requires at least fourfold fewer events.
These are managed allocation-event counts, not memory bytes or headset frame time.
The earlier .NET counter returned zero and was rejected as unsupported evidence.
At this checkpoint, four older echo, speech, world-time and weather allocation
checks still used that counter. Their measurement gap, plus four additional
PlayMode checks, is resolved by the later [allocation measurement repair](#verified-allocation-measurement--2026-10-15).

## Verified allocation measurement — 2026-10-15

The allocation checks now use one shared test-only Unity recorder. Each measure
first records a known allocation on the same thread, verifies that it was observed,
then clears the recorder before running the synchronous measured operation.
Separate EditMode and PlayMode controls distinguish allocating and empty work.
Assertions, reporting and delegate construction stay outside the measured span.
This replaces every GetAllocatedBytesForCurrentThread use in the Unity sources;
that .NET counter had returned zero even for known allocating work in this editor.

The repaired check exposed **512 allocations in 512 idle display ticks**. Compiled
IL showed a captured `now`/`this` closure constructed before the idle return.
The idle gate now calls a separate dormant-work method only when needed, preserving
all readiness checks and loading decisions. Compiled IL has no constructor in that
gate, and the same 512 ticks now record **zero allocation events**.

Fresh focused verification passed **38 EditMode / 33 PlayMode**, no failures or
skips, and **204 web tests / two catalog files**. Both recorder controls detect
an allocating operation and zero for empty work. Warm measured loops also record
zero events for 1,000 echo frames, 10,000 speech blocks, 1,000 lighting samples,
1,000 weather samples, 512 dormant-bounds reads and 512 prepared-skin reads.
Environmental admission records zero events across four 4,096-query trials for
each of its direct and batched paths. Its evidence now labels events explicitly
instead of reporting them as bytes. This is current-thread desktop Unity evidence,
not device-wide memory, bytes, frame time or headset acceptance.

The native catalog export and web drift check passed; only RoomDisplayDemand.cs's
source hash changed. IDs, versions, schemas, saved state and browser fixtures are
unchanged. All 3,006 frozen native inputs and 1,121 mirrored C# files match;
14 unrelated files remain byte-for-byte unchanged.

The preceding complete native suites, offline book/headless journeys and unsigned

## Shared saved-object discovery — 2026-10-15

The native `world.objects` fact now searches the saved top-level object directory
by name, ID words and kind. It includes dormant creations without loading,
selecting or moving them. The book's object hierarchy uses this same fact with
search, kind filtering and Previous/Next controls. Existing selection, inspector,
edit revisions, Undo and program execution remain on their original shared paths.

Results contain stable IDs, full names and kinds, ordered by ID. Each page holds
up to three entries and fits the existing 1,024-character program-value budget.
Escaped Unicode names reduce the page size instead of being truncated. The result
reports the durable world ID, offset, total and an opaque continuation. A cursor
binds the metadata generation, normalized filter and workspace/temporary session.
Rename, addition, removal, kind changes and their Undo/Redo invalidate it; a stale
continuation reports unavailable and requires a fresh search. Movement, paint,
geometry and native loading do not invalidate the directory. The book preserves
its inspector when paging and rejects late results from another room/session.

The directory is a cached metadata projection of the authoritative journal,
invalidated only on accepted publication. It does not copy geometry or create a
second saved world. A returned page cannot mutate its cache. Search results are
not edit revisions or proof of native readiness: known targets use
`object.definition` and `object.presence` for those questions. An ID absent from a
page is not evidence that the saved object was deleted.

Fresh verification passed **65 EditMode / 21 PlayMode** checks, no failures/skips,
including the complete current 66-object directory, exact native catalog/program
parity, dormant lookup with no activation, filters/cursor tampering, name escaping,
accepted/rejected edits, history, fork/reload and temporary-boundary behavior.
**2,965 web tests / 314 files**, application and probe TypeScript checks, and the
native catalog drift check passed. The native export adds only `world.objects`;
existing definitions are unchanged. The original book in desktop Chromium replayed
actual native search pages through first/next/previous/select, with no page errors.
Wide and narrow screenshots were inspected. Browser acknowledgements are simulated;
this is not headset or real-provider acceptance.

All 3,014 frozen native inputs and 1,125 mirrored C# files match; 14 unrelated files
remain byte-for-byte unchanged. Evidence: `.quest-evidence/object-discovery/`.
The complete native suites, offline full-app journeys and unsigned Android build
from **7fe48619** remain separate baseline evidence, not reruns of this change.

This is the shared discovery prerequisite for larger worlds. The periodic scene
state and other pickers still use the complete bounded object inventory; they have

headset/provider/signing/deployment/Meta holds remain. V1 is incomplete.

paging; schema constraints disable incompatible choices, including built-in
objects for creation-copy inputs. Merely opening or reading a field changes no
draft, saved object, native view or program. Older clients without the discovery

## Self-contained object inspection — 2026-10-15

Native inspection now includes the selected object's detached saved/runtime
metadata beside its recipe and exact object revision. Known-ID inspection uses
the same projection as ordinary observation without scanning or depending on the
periodic inventory. Dormant and unavailable native views retain saved placement;
inspection does not load them. The wire normalizes movement values consistently
in both locations. Older hosts can still use a matching inventory entry.

The original book and delegated planner share a compact revision basis captured
from the state they reviewed. The inspector works with an empty displayed list,
keeps its exact target, and retains rejected drafts. A newer native observation
does not silently replace the author's reviewed revision. Changes to native
session or temporary-room identity invalidate old leases and prevent applying a
draft in a different room, even when object revisions happen to match.

Modern commands refuse missing required target evidence before dispatch. Nested
capability resources all need guards; an alias is exempt only after its creation
in the same batch. Read-only inspection can discover an exact ID without an edit
guard. Native receipt reconciliation is preserved after target deletion: an old
run ID uses non-authorizing zero revisions, and native receipt handling either
returns the original result or rejects the identity. It cannot become a new edit,
including after Undo restores the target. Legacy hosts retain scene guards.

## Shared object read sets — 2026-10-15

The shared client can now prepare missing exact-object revisions through the
existing native `object.definition` fact. Preparation retains revisions already
reviewed, reads only missing required targets and dispatches no edit unless all
those reads succeed. It does not silently retry stale writes. Session or temporary
room changes stop the preparation; creation aliases and old execution receipts
retain their existing special handling. The same API is available to book and
headless clients, without another object lookup protocol or capability family.

The action catalog retains the revision context of its explicit current-input
reads, including nested construction members. A later periodic observation cannot
replace those reviewed guards. Selection changes and move-handle requests use the
shared preparation path. Construction selection validation now trusts native
membership independently of the displayed inventory; malformed, duplicate,
oversized and inconsistent handle observations still fail validation. Native
selection continues to enforce saved existence, readiness and its own state ID.

Delegated tasks retain successful exact definition reads from their own earlier
receipts, within the same room scope. These are the same receipts supplied to the
planner. Their compact revision evidence can guard a later multi-object command
even with an empty periodic inventory. A newly unavailable definition removes that
prior fact evidence; current explicit observations remain authoritative. The task
does not silently invent an extra read or refresh a guard when it dispatches.

## Observation-independent controls — 2026-10-15

Native observations now report `workspaceReady` from the content binding. The book
settles startup navigation using that signal instead of requiring a book entry in
the displayed object list. False means content is not bound; true does not bypass
runtime gates or promise readiness of every object. Older hosts retain the prior
complete-list fallback. Client validation rejects malformed readiness values.

Schema-generated object fields no longer select the first displayed object or an
example identity. Only a schema restricting the field to one exact ID may supply
that identity automatically. Explicit compatible choices survive variant changes.
Selected construction pieces now reuse the shared exact-ID reference summary and
optional Read control. Names can be read outside the current view without changing
membership, loading objects or reserving an edit revision. Native selection order
and the first-piece origin remain authoritative.

Standalone inspect and motion queries do not carry edit guards. Conflicting old
revision evidence therefore cannot stop a refresh. New writes still require the
exact reviewed targets, and stale/missing guards retain their existing failures.

restores statuses and refuses duplicate work without replay or provider usage.

### 2026-10-15 — Explicit saved identity across native restart

The learner collector and restore handoff no longer use a periodic object array
as proof of saved membership. Startup and clean-finish directory reads preserve
world/session identity and exact sorted IDs; two complete passes detect membership
changes. Temporary, pending and unavailable state is refused. Pose revisions may
advance independently. Resume requires a new native session and the same saved
world/IDs, verifies the profile digest before loading it, and preserves access mode.
Setup failures now close the native transport. Older diagnostic receipts lacking
explicit final identity are refused without modifying their saved files.

A dedicated offline identity mode reopens a copied, closed workspace through the
actual Unity process and shared facts. A command-level guard admits only saved
object-directory reads; it prevents unrelated shared fixtures from changing the
copy. The sole native-source change is this Editor-only restore gate. Production
native behavior, periodic inventory and saved limits are unchanged.

`7ffec49e44c249309e76449e35097c9b` each passed 9 explicit directory requests with empty
periodic object arrays. All 5 object IDs and the durable world ID survived;
the native session changed. Web tests passed **3,051 / 324 files**, application/probe
TypeScript and all eight invalid-mode guards passed. All 3,014 native inputs
match the mirror and all 14 unrelated files remain byte-identical. Evidence:
`.quest-evidence/sparse-learner-resume/`. An initial stale-mirror preflight and two
initial runs that still executed shared audio checks remain recorded but are not
accepted as read-only restart evidence; the command guard and corrected runs
supersede them.

This is native saved-identity acceptance, not paid-lesson or learner-profile replay,
headset, complete-property restoration or Store acceptance. Earlier full Headless
and Original Book evidence remains at `618099bf`; broad native suites and Android
compilation remain at their documented checkpoints. Next: make production object

semantics. Preserve the authoritative paged directory and exact target reads.
Do not raise saved capacity or claim new provider/device acceptance from these runs.


## 2026-10-15 — Bounded production object overview

Modern native hosts now advertise `objectOverview.v1`. Their periodic `objects`
array is a context of at most four saved IDs: book, Maestro, the current selection
and the last inspected object, deduplicated and omitting missing objects. IDs are
chosen before projection; the runtime does not build the complete observed
inventory and then truncate it. Hosts without saved-directory support retain the
older observation contract.

The matching metadata explicitly says `scope:"context"` and `exhaustive:false`.
It remains non-exhaustive even in a small world. Omission never establishes
absence, deletion, visibility, inactivity or native unloading. `workspaceReady`
remains separate. The shared client rejects missing/contradictory markers,
unadvertised metadata and over-limit arrays. Exact directory and target reads,
self-contained inspection and reviewed revisions remain available outside the
context. Planner and final-reply guidance explain the same semantics; the new
shared contract is checked for runtime-independent imports so Functions can use
it too. No secondary browser world was introduced.

Verified in `.quest-evidence/bounded-object-overview/`:

  projection, detached observations, selection changes without deletion, complete
  saved-directory discovery and inspection outside the context. Allocation counts
  were **55 with 5 saved objects and 55 with 66**; this is a projection measurement,
  not a general large-world performance claim.
- A real PlayMode wire observation was accepted by the shared TypeScript client,
  including its separately inspected object absent from the context.
- Production Headless run **2fb9d910655247ff8fdb27e5841581e7** passed with
  **2,183 observations / 2,177 prepared read sets**. Task steering used 15 scripted
  responses through the real chat/verifier/task/native path; that provider
  transport recorded zero network attempts. Stop, revise, continue, reopening,
  programs, Undo/Redo and motion evidence passed; motion retained 175 samples.
- Production Original Book run **c8980f6d17c349809659ae397211d87c** passed with
  **989 native requests / 3,194 observations / 27 scripted planning calls**.
  Visible native forms read seven objects through explicit directory/target
  evidence; human edits, stale-edit refusal and reload without replay passed.
  Two captured catalog/form views were visually inspected without layout defects.
- Both journeys used the actual production projection, **without SparseObjectView**.
  Every recorded state declared the contract; the largest observed context was
  three objects, within the enforced limit of four. The separate prior empty-view

- **3,064 web tests / 325 files**, application/probe TypeScript, catalog provenance
