## 2026-10-15 — procedural native reservations

Procedural recipe preparation now reserves native instance, part and generated
custom-mesh source-vertex costs before allocating Unity objects. The reservation
is shared across current, closing and staged workspaces and persists through
disabled geometry and deferred destruction. Its 128-instance / 512-part /
524,288-custom-vertex bounds are independent of saved-world quotas and leave
bounded replacement headroom. They are logical safeguards, not measured Quest
memory or frame-time limits.

A refused edit leaves persistence, accepted geometry, running recipe playback
and Undo/Redo untouched. Failed dormant activation keeps the saved object and
can retry through the same loading path. No automatic eviction or saved-object
deletion was added. Shared catalog facts `runtime.proceduralBudget` and
`runtime.proceduralReservation` expose copied runtime ownership and phases to
both human and agent inspection; reads cannot allocate or load geometry.

Verification for this checkpoint is recorded in
`.quest-evidence/procedural-reservations/`. Focused native checks cover competing
preparations, each budget dimension, wide cost arithmetic, cancellation,
deferred destruction, exact ownership, failed persistence/admission, later
retry, Undo/Redo and dormant activation. Actual Unity fact output is retained in
`test-fixtures/browser/proceduralReservations.json` and validated by web tests.
Accepted evidence: **36 EditMode / 38 PlayMode** checks (35 initial EditMode checks
plus one added aggregate-budget case; the five resource cases were rerun),
**3,069 web tests / 326 files**, application/probe TypeScript and generated-catalog
provenance all passed. Headless `a735b1f9f58a4ca89ca1a7fccdeb8c9d` passed
2,189 observations / 2,182 explicit read sets with 15 scripted provider
responses and zero provider-network attempts. Original Book
`fc371ad25fcb4d319eb70e1ce14aff51` passed 985 native requests /
3,139 observations / 27 scripted planning calls, with no browser errors,
human-edit preservation, stale agent-edit refusal and reload without replay.
These are offline desktop integrations, not paid-provider or physical-device proof.
All 4,260 frozen inputs, 3,024 native source/mirror inputs and 14 unrelated
files were checked. The two added catalog facts are the only definition changes.

Saved capacity, the 25-metre position bound and whole-document transaction costs
remain unchanged. Further saved/runtime separation is required for collisions,
object instances, ink, light/window and simulation costs. Existing collision
resource diagnostics report ownership and source costs; they do not themselves
enforce a process-wide collision budget. No larger-world, paid-provider, headset,
packaging or Store acceptance is claimed. V1 remains incomplete.


## Authored collision resource admission (2026-10-15)

Compound, terrain and rigid imported collision now reserve independent native
capacity before allocation: 256 retained entries, 2,048 colliders and 1,048,576
source triangles. Costs follow preparation, accepted/disabled geometry,
replacement overlap and deferred destruction across workspaces. Compound and
terrain leases retain separately owned meshes until those meshes are destroyed;
shared imported meshes retain their separate model ownership. Rigid imported
collision remains limited to 32 meshes / 50,000 triangles per object. These
bounds provide logical replacement headroom, not measured Quest RAM, PhysX
cooked size or frame-time guarantees.

Admission failure preserves accepted geometry, saved bytes, revisions and
Undo/Redo. Dormant activation keeps its saved target and can retry after capacity
is released. Unexpected actual geometry exceeding its reservation rejects
preparation and remains charged through rollback. There is no automatic eviction
or saved-data deletion. User and agent inspection share the passive
`runtime.collisionBudget` fact; existing owner/totals fact schemas are unchanged.
Their descriptions now explain enforcement and owned-mesh retirement.

/ 327 files**, application/probe TypeScript and catalog provenance passed.

compound/terrain/imported replacement, deferred destruction, actual-cost
mismatch and dormant activation retry. Actual native budget/owner output in
`test-fixtures/browser/collisionAdmission.json` is validated on the web side.

## Native object admission (2026-10-16)

User-created native roots now reserve process-wide capacity before production
allocation: 128 primitives, drawings, assemblies and imported-model hosts across
workspaces. Private candidates, disabled objects and roots waiting for Unity
destruction retain their reservation. Saved capacity remains 64 creations plus
book/Maestro. Built-in book/Maestro and tool roots are excluded; meshes, images,
ink, lamps, windows, collision and simulation have separate costs. This logical
bound does not establish Quest memory or frame-time capacity.

Preparation transfers the exact target and kind to its constructed root. Failed
batches and storage writes release unused candidates, while capacity refusal
preserves saved bytes, revisions and Undo/Redo. Dormant activation retains its
saved identity and can retry. A fresh workspace at capacity retains its starter
content as dormant instead of leaving a partially created scene. Never-active
and unpublished roots release their slot after actual destruction even when
Unity does not invoke component OnDestroy.

Predictive retention now uses the same conservative visual envelope as dormant
display admission, including disabled/rest geometry. Exact rendered observation
still honors active renderer state and camera layers. This prevents an object
from repeatedly unloading and immediately reloading ahead of unrelated groups.
A deterministic regression verifies the shared envelope, stable root identity,
unrelated retirement and release after the viewer moves away. The original
failure and diagnostic traces are preserved with this checkpoint.

Human and agent programs share passive `runtime.objectBudget` and
`runtime.objectReservation` facts. Ready means the host was constructed, not
published identity, visible geometry, model readiness or running physics. The
web validates native-captured phase and owner output in
`test-fixtures/browser/nativeObjectReservations.json`.

Appearance image entry/owner limits now remain fixed at 128 / 132 instead of
scaling with saved definition/object quotas. The current per-workspace 64 MiB
image limit is unchanged. Image decoding, preview temporaries and deferred
texture destruction still require shared lifetime accounting before expansion;
this change does not claim a global texture budget.

15 scripted responses with zero provider-network attempts.

requests / 3,171 observations / 27 scripted
planning calls, preserving human edits, refusing stale writes and reloading

## Shared image lifetime admission (2026-10-16)

Appearance caches and local/chat image verification now share a process-wide
ledger before reading encoded payloads or allocating native textures. Admission
bounds are 128 texture reservations, 128 MiB of conservative native texture
source estimates, 128 MiB of decoder scratch estimates, four payload owners and
64 MiB of encoded payload reservations. The FIFO decoder admits at most one
native decode attempt per frame across workspaces. Existing saved quotas and
per-workspace appearance cache limits are unchanged.

Orientation correction reserves both native textures, readable texture sources,
pixel arrays and metadata-copy estimates. Native textures remain charged until
Unity destruction completes; a decoded image that exceeds its inspected cost
fails and retains the actual larger charge through rollback. Cancelled reads
and queued decodes keep their payload reservation until work drains. Closing a
workspace cannot publish a late texture. Completed import previews retain only
bounded metadata instead of keeping their encoded pixels. Admission failure
preserves existing textures, saved bytes, revisions and Undo/Redo; explicit
retry succeeds after capacity becomes available. Separate workspaces still
validate their own private image files and own separate physical cache entries.

Shared `runtime.imageResources` and indexed `runtime.imageResource` facts expose
these logical reservations to user and agent programs. Existing image facts
retain current-workspace semantics. The new reservation owner identifies the
originating request, not every current cache holder; existing image-owner facts
provide that holder list. The committed browser fixture is actual native output
from the accepted orientation/deferred-destruction test.

Accepted evidence: **54 EditMode / 33 PlayMode**
image, appearance and catalog checks passed with no skips; **3,086
web tests / 329 files**, both TypeScript checks and catalog provenance
passed. Native regressions independently exercise texture count, texture bytes,
decoder scratch and payload count/bytes, plus cancellation, workspace closure,
preview cleanup, save stability and retry. Existing eight-orientation pixel and
alpha assertions run through the shared decoder.

Full offline Headless `88cafdecfaf5420f840e636cb6c15f46` passed
2,187 observations / 2,178 explicit read sets,
177 motion samples and 15 scripted responses with zero
provider-network attempts. Original Book `c3894f70f60b44b9857326d4fc437a2a` passed
985 requests / 3,163 observations / 27 scripted
planning calls, with preserved human edits, stale-write refusal, no browser
errors and no replay on reload. Both use the bounded production context.
All 4,288 frozen source/fixture/config inputs, 3,046
native source/mirror inputs and 14 unrelated files were verified.
Evidence: `.quest-evidence/image-lifetime-admission/`.

The first Book attempt timed out at its initial browser handshake, before any
lesson request or native observation. Its blank capture and zero-error log are
preserved. A standalone fixture loaded in 5.5 seconds; the unchanged full rerun
passed. The initial startup cause remains unestablished, not a repaired product
defect. Details: `book-startup-failure.json` in this checkpoint.

These limits are source-derived logical estimates, not measured process or GPU
RAM. Decoder internals, driver copies and managed buffers awaiting garbage
collection are not measured; embedded model textures, browser/camera textures
and the system placeholder are outside this ledger. One large decode can still
hitch, so one per frame is not a frame-time guarantee. Broad Unity suites,
Android compilation, paid providers, headset performance and Store acceptance
were not rerun. Transactional ink/light/window preparation, simulation admission
and transaction/storage/extent work remain before increasing saved capacity.
V1 remains incomplete.


## Transactional native lamps (2026-10-16)

Authored local lamps now prepare detached, disabled native candidates before a
save or journal/history acceptance. Unchanged definitions reuse their native
Light, including when a recipe replaces an attachment. Removal does not reserve
a replacement. Ordinary edits, visual-only edits, Undo/Redo, initial creation
and dormant activation transfer the same exact-target/source preparation.
Unrelated reconciliation cannot allocate a missing peer's lamps after acceptance.

Temporary-room discard now prepares the saved destination before ending its
paired temporary memory session or swapping journals. This also admits restored
native roots, recipes, compound/terrain geometry and changed imported collision
through their existing preparation paths. Admission/readiness refusal leaves the
temporary source and memory available for retry; it does not promise that every
restore can proceed immediately while other resources or actions are active.

A separate process-wide ledger reserves at most eight native lamps and twelve
shadow faces across preparation, ready views, disabled roots and deferred
retirement. Point shadows reserve six faces; spot shadows reserve one. Cancelled
private candidates and replaced lamps remain charged until Unity destroys their
roots, including never-active roots. These constants are independent of the
unchanged saved quotas (two lamps/object, four/room, six shadow faces).
`runtime.lightBudget` and `runtime.lightReservation` provide the same passive,
typed diagnostics to user and agent programs. Existing facts/actions/events and
adapters are unchanged. The browser fixture is captured native lifecycle output.

Verification: **1,311 EditMode / 1,690 PlayMode** passed, with
**3 optional private-asset skips** in the full native run. The focused
lighting/temporary-room PlayMode run passed **110** tests. New regressions cover
separate lamp/shadow saturation, wrong actual shadow cost, cross-workspace
reservations, exact-source transfer, disabled/deferred lifetime, failed batches
and saves, history and temporary-discard refusal/retry, recipe reattachment,
missing peer views and dormant activation. **3,091 web tests / 330 files**, both
TypeScript checks and catalog provenance passed. All **4,300 frozen inputs**,
**3,056 native source/mirror files** and **14 unrelated files** were verified.
The initial pre-export catalog check correctly rejected the stale committed
manifest; the regenerated artifact passed the full suite. Evidence:
`.quest-evidence/authored-visual-admission/`.

This is logical allocation admission, not measured GPU memory, shadow rendering
quality or Quest frame time. Unity can retain internal shadow render targets
beyond component destruction. Sun and other system lights are excluded.
No Android build, paid-provider request, headset check, signing, deployment or
Meta action ran. Full offline Headless/Book journeys were not rerun for this
lamp checkpoint; their most recent evidence remains pinned to `9f38abca` above.
Transactional spatial/surface ink and passthrough windows remain next, followed
by simulation admission and transaction/storage/spatial-extent work before saved
capacity expansion. Per-entity real-room collisions remain independent of virtual
terrain and presentation. V1 is incomplete.


## Transactional native ink (2026-10-16)

Spatial drawings, surface ink and live pencil previews now reserve and prepare
geometry/materials before room acceptance. Immediate-save authoring also prepares
before persistence. Unchanged strokes and patch roots are reused; color, size,
drawing-permission and pose edits do not rebuild ink. Recipe replacement detaches
and reattaches the same patch roots so their ink and windows survive. Retained
window masks update scale on patch resize. Scanned-ink anchors add no renderer.
See [the ink contract](QUEST_SURFACE_DRAWING.md#native-ink-preparation-and-lifetime-2026-10-16).

Independent process-wide limits account for 128 surface roots, 384 strokes and
196,608 reserved vertices across workspaces, private candidates, previews,
inactive hosts and deferred destruction. Failed preparation or immediate saving
preserves accepted ink, saved bytes and history. Undo/Redo, temporary discard and
dormant activation preserve retry after refusal. A failed preview retains all
captured stroke samples, even if its visible preview is incomplete. Shared
`runtime.inkBudget` and `runtime.inkReservation` expose copied ownership and
lifetime facts to both users and agents. A ready host may still be a private
candidate; diagnostics do not claim publication or visibility.

Full desktop verification passed **1,314 EditMode / 1,698 PlayMode** tests,
with **3 optional private-asset skips**. Eleven new native regressions cover
independent budgets across worlds, projection before allocation, actual-cost
mismatch, never-active cleanup, partial initialization, source-exact transfer,
failed batches and saves, unchanged window/ink reuse, history/temporary recovery,
dormant activation, preview retry/discard and deferred native lifetime. The first
focused run caught and corrected an unwanted renderer on scanned-ink anchors.
The expanded focused run passed 149/150 PlayMode checks; its failing new test had
injected storage failure into the older queued-save entry point. It now exercises
the surface editor's immediate-save path and passes in the full run.

**3,096 web tests / 331 files**, app/probe TypeScript and catalog provenance
passed. Only the two ink diagnostics were added to the catalog; existing
capabilities are unchanged. A native lifecycle capture validates web facts and
programs. All **4,314 frozen inputs**, **3,068
native source/mirror files** and **14 unrelated files** were
verified. Evidence: `.quest-evidence/ink-admission/`.

This is logical allocation admission, not measured GPU memory or Quest frame
time. System artwork and selection outlines are excluded. No Android build,

## Transactional native windows (2026-10-16)

Authored passthrough windows now prepare detached, inactive masks and their shared
quad/material bundle before journal acceptance or immediate-save persistence.
Preparation cannot request passthrough. Adoption applies accepted visibility before
requesting the underlay, so a fully hidden opening cannot briefly reveal the room.
Stable window IDs reuse masks across reveal, shape, size and anchor edits. Rebinding
detaches retained masks before removing their previous surface. Removing the last
window also releases its native bundle without reserving a replacement.
See [the window contract](QUEST_WORLD_AUTHORING.md#native-window-preparation-and-lifetime-2026-10-16).

Independent process-wide limits reserve 32 masks and 32 shared bundles across
workspaces, private candidates, inactive hosts and deferred destruction. Saved
quotas remain four windows/object and sixteen/room. Exact-target/source transfer,
failed batches and immediate saves, Undo/Redo, temporary discard and dormant
activation share the preparation path. Explicit visual-only edits can recover
missing surface anchors before acceptance; unrelated reconciliation cannot
allocate a missing peer's windows. Failure preserves accepted masks and their
properties, source, saved bytes and history.

Shared `runtime.windowBudget` and `runtime.windowReservation` expose detached,
passive owner/lifetime facts to both users and agents. `ready` may include an
unpublished native candidate; `active` describes the host GameObject. Neither
certifies visibility or working passthrough. Reads never allocate masks, evict
content or request the underlay. Compositor resources and internal rendering
allocations are excluded; these logical limits do not measure GPU memory or
establish Quest performance.

Full desktop verification passed **1,317 EditMode / 1,706 PlayMode** tests,
with **3 optional private-asset skips**. Eleven new native regressions cover
independent mask/bundle saturation, exact-source transfer, never-active cleanup,
stable mask reattachment, failed batches and immediate saves, temporary/history
recovery, dormant activation, missing-peer isolation and private/hidden window
presentation. The focused run passed **20 EditMode / 158 PlayMode** checks.
The full run also verifies a refused property-only save preserves an existing
window's mask, reveal value and passthrough request.

**3,101 web tests / 332 files**, app/probe TypeScript and catalog provenance
passed. A captured native lifecycle fixture validates the same typed web facts
and programs. Existing capabilities are unchanged; only two window diagnostics
were added. All **4,328 frozen inputs**, **3,080 native source/mirror
files** and **14 unrelated files** were verified. Evidence:
`.quest-evidence/window-admission/`.

No Android build, headset, paid-provider, signing, deployment or Meta action ran.
Full offline Headless/Book journeys were not rerun for this window checkpoint;
their most recent evidence remains pinned to `9f38abca`. Per-entity real-room
collisions remain independent of authored terrain and presentation. Simulation

## Transactional native physics (2026-10-16)

Authored native bodies and their PhysicsMaterials now reserve independent
capacity before root construction. Enabled physical connections reserve potential
joint slots before acceptance and immediate saving. Ordinary edits reuse accepted
bodies/slots; disabled or removed connections need no replacement capacity.
See [the physics contract](QUEST_WORLD_AUTHORING.md#native-physics-preparation-and-lifetime-2026-10-16).

Separate process-wide limits admit **128 authored bodies/materials** and
**32 potential joints**, across workspaces, private preparation, inactive hosts
and deferred destruction. Paused, broken and unaligned enabled connections retain
their slot. Native joint replacement waits for the old Joint to be destroyed,
preventing overlap within one slot. A body lease follows a private root before
Rigidbody construction and can reclaim failed, never-active partial builds.
The Rigidbody and owned material must both be gone before its slot is released.

Exact object ID/kind and connection-source/owner checks guard transfer. Initial
startup, ordinary edits, immediate saves, history, temporary-room recovery and
dormant activation share preparation. A saturated limit or failed save preserves
accepted source, native views, saved bytes and history. Unrelated reconciliation
cannot repair an unprepared missing peer connection. Explicit edits and activation
can retry when capacity becomes available. Per-entity real-room collision policy,
accepted virtual ground, physics intent, ownership and throw semantics are intact.

Shared `runtime.physicsBudget` and `runtime.physicsReservation` provide detached,
passive ownership facts to human and agent programs. Potential joint reservations
can be ready with no allocated Joint; an existing Rigidbody/Joint can be kinematic,
paused or retiring. Neither ready, active nor allocated certifies simulation.
Reads cannot start physics or mutate saved state.

Final-source verification passed **1,322 EditMode / 1,714 PlayMode tests**,
with the same **3 optional private-asset skips**. Thirteen new native regressions
cover independent capacity, exact transfer, partial/never-active construction,
failed saves and late batches, live-joint preservation, pause/deferred replacement,
history and temporary discard, dormant activation, missing-peer isolation and
passive lifecycle capture. Focused checks passed **21 EditMode / 155 PlayMode**.
The initial focused run passed before the final partial-build cleanup fix; the
fresh focused and full runs both include that fix.

**3,106 web tests / 333 files**, application/probe TypeScript and catalog provenance
passed. The browser fixture is captured from real Unity body/potential-joint
lifecycle output. All existing actions/events/facts/adapters and limits are
unchanged; exactly two passive physics facts were added. All **4,340 frozen
inputs**, **3,090 native source/mirror files** and **14 unrelated files** were
verified. Evidence: `.quest-evidence/physics-admission/`. PR discussion/reviews and
four inline findings were read; current source and regression tests already
address the reported cancellation, Live-origin and backup issues.

These are logical authored allocation limits, not measurements of PhysX memory,
solver cost, frame time or Quest performance. System book/avatar/tools, tracked
pushers, broadphase/contact data and simulation/query work are excluded. Saved
quotas remain 64 creations and 16 connections. The next admission work must bound
live media and complete physical/query work units without silently omitting
relevant water, breaking volume conservation, or letting connected bodies move
on incomplete input. Transaction/storage/spatial-extent bounds remain necessary
before increasing saved capacity. The source-grounded follow-on audit is in
`next-source-audit.json` in this checkpoint's evidence directory.

## Transactional native liquids (2026-10-16)

Containers now prepare both their liquid surface and potential stream before
acceptance, including empty vessels. Filling, physical pouring, rain and cavity
edits reuse those native instances. Separate process-wide admission allows
**32 liquid owners** across workspaces, private preparation, inactive hosts and
deferred destruction. Saved capacity remains **16 containers per room**.
See the [shared lifetime contract](QUEST_WORLD_AUTHORING.md#liquid-preparation-and-lifetime-2026-10-16).

Each owner holds one surface mesh, two materials and both renderers. Failed
preparation, never-active owners and destroyed native roots can be reclaimed;
slots remain charged until their Unity objects are gone. Exact target,
container-source and previous-owner checks protect transfer. Ordinary edits,
immediate saves, Undo/Redo, temporary discard and dormant activation use the
same preparation. Admission refusal or failed immediate saving preserves
accepted quantity, native mesh/material, saved bytes and history. Unrelated
reconciliation does not recreate a missing peer view. The older queued-save
entry point retains its existing persistence policy.

Live-medium capture now uses the independent native capacity. An inconsistent
capacity reports unavailable rather than silently returning a prefix. Missing
native water remains a spatial dependency for affected movement and physics;
it cannot become dry space or silently borrow an outer pool. Existing live
amount, cavity relevance, terrain and per-participant real-room policies remain
authoritative. Empty and distant missing vessels retain their existing handling.
Native ownership does not start physics or alter transfer/publication semantics.

`runtime.liquidBudget` and `runtime.liquidReservation` add detached passive facts
for human and agent programs. Ready/active/allocated describes ownership and
native roots, not water presence or simulation readiness. The shared browser
fixture comes from actual Unity private/ready/inactive/retiring lifecycle output.
Exactly two facts were added; existing actions, events, facts, adapters and limits
remain unchanged.

## Stable free-body physics work admission (2026-10-16)

Potential liquid-force work now has process-wide stable reservations independent
of saved quotas and native allocations. Eligible free members of each connected
physics group are admitted atomically. New demand cannot evict unrelated
accepted physics. Collider-count or copied-owner changes invalidate old grants;
retry can update them only when the whole requested group fits. The separate
limits are 128 grants and 524,288 logical units, with bounded per-body
occupancy/media/proof allowances. This does not measure CPU time or engine work.
See the [shared contract](QUEST_WORLD_AUTHORING.md#free-body-physics-work-admission-2026-10-16).

Refused work holds affected bodies before gravity and liquid forces, reports a
specific waiting reason and remains retryable. It releases pending spatial
preparation rather than retaining an old throw. A failed dormancy check no
longer overwrites an existing capacity explanation with an empty error. Pause,
hand/animation ownership, inactive hosts and destruction release potential
work. Native lifetime release stops the body immediately, rejects motion
commands and prevents reacquisition while the GameObject awaits destruction. Returning capacity or read-only queries cannot restart paused physics.
The existing per-entity real-room policy, accepted ground, missing-water
semantics, connection ownership and saved quantities remain authoritative.

The typed catalog adds `runtime.physicsWorkBudget` and
`runtime.physicsWorkReservation`; native capture checks current, stale, resized
and released grants through the web validator. A current grant is only a work
reservation, not proof that the body or its complete group can simulate.

private-asset skips**. Eight new native checks cover logical demand bounds,
whole-group refusal, unrelated physics continuity, demand growth/shrink,
pending momentum cancellation, denied water sampling, retry, pause and ownership,
retirement before destruction, and detached current/stale/resized/released
observations. Focused checks passed **41 EditMode / 86 PlayMode** cases.

real-room and virtual-ground policies remain unchanged.

## Complete liquid flow work admission (2026-10-16)

Pouring, scooping and rain now reserve a complete room pass before transfer
queries or quantity changes. Demand includes empty receivers and missing views;
resizing credits the old reservation and cannot evict peers or admit a prefix.

gravity, while unrelated dry objects can continue and hands/animations retain
ownership. Per-entity real-room and virtual-ground policies are unchanged.

invalidates rain exposure. Skipped time cannot accumulate intake. Failed saving
reverts the entire unfinished episode and requires an explicit physics restart.
Completed events copy accepted amounts/identity and release write access before
callbacks. A rule may edit the room or begin new flow; remaining old-source events
stop, and old cleanup cannot hide fresh streams or clear the new episode.

`runtime.flowWorkReservation`; live pouring/scooping/rain observations document
waiting and its reason. These detached diagnostics cannot admit, start or publish

## Contact source and temporal continuity (2026-10-16)

participants still deliver. Nested sampling or a changed room supersedes the
remaining old batch. Avatar pose comparison retains captured world positions

callback batch; tracking, exact pose, geometry, ownership and source edits retain

## Liquid quantity execution by complete group (2026-10-16)

The native quantity pass now enumerates each complete prepared liquid group for
pouring, immersion, scooping and rain. Stream and scoop candidates come only from
that group's members. A separate ready circuit no longer enumerates the waiting
circuit's containers. Required solids and rain cover still participate in spatial
proofs and PhysX queries; sharing a read-only roof does not merge quantities.

The complete group is captured after native input preparation. If any member has
changed or is unready, no prefix of that plan executes. Each pass rebuilds the
groups from current geometry, including empty receivers and transitive
dependencies. Moving a receiver between circuits therefore changes the candidate
set before the next quantity pass. Pour, scoop and rain phase order and the
single host journal remain: live quantities publish together, with one Undo,
and failed publication rolls back unaccepted changes.

The previous room-wide loops failed all three new native reproductions. For two
independent two-vessel circuits, the same 28 stream segments made 112 candidate
visits before and 56 after; immersion visits fell from 8 to 4 and scoop visits
from 16 to 8. With one unavailable circuit, stream candidate visits fell from 56
to 28 while the ready circuit still conserved its water. A moved receiver formed
a three-vessel circuit plus one isolated source, with 10 scoop visits; returning
it restored two complete pairs and 8 visits. These are instrumented visits in
actual native loops, not frame-time or CPU measurements.

All three new native reproductions pass after the change. Expanded focused
verification passed **44 EditMode / 182 PlayMode tests**, including
pouring, scooping, rain, spatial readiness, rollback, contact continuity and
coordinated physics/liquid scheduling. **3,146 web tests / 345 files**, app/probe
TypeScript and catalog provenance passed. All catalog definitions are unchanged;
the four changed runtime source hashes were refreshed.

The last full Unity suite remains pinned to **98c4808d**: **1,330 EditMode /
1,799 PlayMode passed**, with three unchanged optional private-asset skips.
Full offline Book/Headless journeys remain pinned to **9f38abca**, and Android
compilation to **7fe48619**. No new headset, real-provider, acoustic, Android,
signing, deployment or Meta acceptance is claimed. Local evidence:
.quest-evidence/flow-group-execution/.

This step deliberately retains whole-host work allowances and physics/liquid
coupling. Discovery, input preparation, stream cleanup and publication still have
room-wide work. The discovery allowance is implemented below. Accounting for
geometry/proofs and engine work, preserving fairness through split/merge, and
assigning execution allowances to actual complete groups remain required before

Saved/native limits, catalog definitions and saved formats are unchanged.
The full v1 remains incomplete; device/provider/release holds remain in place.


## Admitted liquid dependency discovery (2026-10-16)

Liquid dependency discovery now has its own logical allowance on the shared
simulation clock. The scheduler grants that allowance atomically with the
host's liquid work and its dependent physics. Independent discovery and execution
reservations cannot hold opposite halves while waiting for each other. Contact
sampling remains independent. Physics may register this complete demand before
the liquid pass discovers any geometry.

Demand uses the canonical saved object count and registered vessel count,
including dormant objects and empty receivers. It does not need collision reads,
a copied room document or native acquisition. Changing either count or the owner
invalidates the old readiness; a rejected increase restores the prior booked
charge and does not evict peers. Existing work, saved and native caps remain.
The discovery limit is 524,288 logical units; its inventory ceiling follows the
existing 64 creations plus book/Maestro. This is not acceptance of a maximum-size
native world, nor a CPU, allocation, PhysX or frame-time measurement.

Admission precedes the liquid pass's collision/cavity capture, candidate queries
and native input preparation. One collision snapshot is reused inside that
synchronous discovery only. Every later pass captures current placements again;
it is never reused across loading, physics or another liquid tick. In the two
native circuits with a moved real obstacle, collision-extent reads fall from 60
to 10 per discovery, while the same six probes still visit 60 candidates. The
next pass detects the moved obstruction, spills only the affected stream and
preserves conservation. This reduces repeated bounds reads, not all room-wide
discovery, proof or engine work.

The shared typed runtime.flowDiscoveryBudget fact reports the step, registered
requests, booked grants, booked units and limits. User and agent programs use
the same definition and strict record validation. Booked work is not completed
discovery or native readiness; it can be stale until refreshed. Passive reads
cannot capture geometry, load native content, advance the clock or alter water.

Both original native failures were reproduced before the fix: discovery ran
under a fully occupied discovery allowance and the same bounds were read for
every probe. Four native scenarios now cover cold loading under continuing
pressure, real moved-obstacle freshness, inventory growth with an unsuccessful
grant resize, and physics preparing before liquid discovery. Pressure claims
are deliberately logical scheduler competitors; they do not represent a tested
number of simultaneously provisioned worlds.

Verification completed **1,332/1,332 EditMode** tests. The full PlayMode run
finished with **1,805 passed, two failures and three optional private-asset skips**.
All four discovery scenarios passed. One older resizing test expected a peer
grant to remain current after its shared room inventory grew. Its identity and
booked values were unchanged, but discovery was correctly stale. The corrected
test checks that distinction and that shrinking back restores the original
receipt. No production source changed after the full run.

A focused follow-up passed **30/30 PlayMode tests**, including
discovery, quantity execution, allowance admission and the unchanged acoustic
class. The original retained-wall/audio-reset failure reported 0 played samples
instead of 19,200; its log includes audio configuration changes. The unchanged
retry passed, but the cause remains unestablished and the original full run is
not green. The three unchanged skips require optional private models outside CI.

The initial web run timed out in one existing headless room-journey case at its
unchanged five-second limit. That file passed all 25 tests unchanged in isolation.
The full rerun with four workers passed **3,150 tests / 346 files**, followed by
app/probe TypeScript and catalog provenance. No assertion or timeout was weakened.
The new fact is exported from native code; captured native values pass the
shared web reader and program typing.

## Discovery before native preparation and execution (2026-10-16)

The preserved old-code regression confirmed that a cold liquid receiver could
not begin loading while quantity execution capacity was occupied: discovery
never ran. The runtime now admits discovery independently. Required native
inputs can prepare while execution is queued, and a fresh synchronous capture
runs after loading before quantities change.

Discovery grants are chosen first on the shared opportunity clock. Moving
physics and liquid execution still require their complete coupled allowances
and current discovery readiness. An unavailable prerequisite leaves execution
queued and preserves valid momentum. Input waiting retains service age and does
not refund an already booked grant during that opportunity. Cross-stage
dependency edges are explicitly refused, preventing discovery from waiting on
the execution capacity that needs its inputs.

The first broader run passed six EditMode tests and 36 PlayMode tests but failed
three. Two assertions still assumed the old single queue; they now distinguish
queued discovery from unbooked execution while retaining their loading and
recovery checks. The third exposed an implementation bug: after a publication
callback started fresh nested flow, the outer pass deferred that new request.
A preparation-version guard now lets only the owning pass defer execution.
The corrected focused run passed all six EditMode and 39 PlayMode tests.
An additional explicit cross-stage rejection is included in the full run.

The full native sweep passed all 1,334 EditMode tests and 1,811 PlayMode tests, with 3
optional private-asset skips and no failures. The retained-wall DSP/reset regression
passed in this run; that does not identify the historical later configuration event or
resolve acoustic-quality/SDK issues. The full offline web suite passed 3,156 tests
across 349 files; app/probe TypeScript and catalog provenance passed. Current full-run
native captures were copied to the shared web fixtures and checked again before
delivery. All 3,168 frozen native inputs match the tested mirror. No device, paid
provider, Android, signing, deployment or Meta operation ran.

The shared catalog describes the stages without changing fact IDs or record

The cold-loading case uses a real native receiver and a logical pressure claim;
the separate nine-room reproduction uses actual native resource owners.
All nine valid rooms can now discover inputs even when one room waits to execute.
Their conservative quantity charges and all saved/native limits remain unchanged.

## Consumed simulation work ledger — 2026-10-16

Before splitting execution grants by complete group, a native regression
demonstrated that removing a host after an actual liquid transfer immediately
made its consumed allowance available again. The receiver had already gained
liquid; a second request then obtained capacity that should remain charged.
The old production sources fail that test at the no-refund assertion.

The scheduler now records each request's largest consumed allowance in the
current opportunity and retains it after removal. Requested growth must fit
alongside existing grants and retained consumption. Shrinking, splitting,
double removal or retiring native owners cannot refund consumed work.
An unused reservation can still release its capacity. The next clock
opportunity clears consumed charges. Discovery, free-body physics, medium
forces, quantity execution and contact sampling use this common accounting.

The first focused run passed 11 EditMode and one native PlayMode test. The
first broader run passed 19 EditMode and 51 PlayMode tests with 11 failures.
Ten came from pressure helpers counting active requests while disregarding
consumed charges retained after earlier native work. Their fill conditions now
use committed ledger capacity, preserving the same admission/refusal checks.
The remaining failure caught a real contract error: the combined diagnostic
exceeded the program reader's 1,024-character budget. The fact now selects one
resource per read, leaving that shared limit intact.

The fact is runtime.simulationWorkLedger, with one required resource argument
and step/resource/committed/consumed/retired/limit fields. Native tests read it
through the actual program catalog; web tests use captured native receipts.
It is passive and does not collect, admit or perform work. Existing active
request facts retain their meaning; a removed request's consumed allowance
can outlive it until the next opportunity.

The full native suite passed 1,345 EditMode tests and 1,813 PlayMode tests, with 3
optional private-asset skips and no failures. The full offline web suite passed 3,160
tests in 350 files; app/probe TypeScript and catalog provenance passed. The full-run
native ledger receipts replaced the focused captures and passed the shared reader
checks again. All 3,176 frozen native inputs match the verification mirror. This is
desktop validation; no device, paid-provider, Android, signing, deployment or Meta
operation ran.

The ledger is an allowance record, not an operation counter or performance
claim. Repeated internal use of one reservation remains idempotent; the
automatic clocks separately govern quantity/contact cadence. Independent
complete-group grants, phase-fresh closure and persistent canonical member
service records remain open. This increment does not solve those by relabeling
host reservations or reducing their costs. Saved/native limits and per-actor
real-room participation are unchanged. Device, provider, audio-quality and
Store release gates remain open.

## Room save inspection and publication guard — 2026-10-17

A measured room-save bottleneck remained after sparse native preparation.
VersionedRoomFile previously decoded the existing primary twice per save.
RoomStorage now explicitly opts into a one-entry validation-outcome cache keyed
by SHA-256 of a fresh bounded read. It does not trust timestamps or length, retain
a mutable document, bypass candidate validation or assume newly serialized bytes
have passed the read path. Other stores remain uncached by default because their
validators can depend on changing catalog state.

The second inspection also honors a newer-format result. Previously a newer
primary appearing during candidate validation could be copied as unreadable and
overwritten. The regression failed against the old implementation and passes now:
publication stops, primary and backup survive, and only the pending candidate is
removed. Other tests cover same-metadata replacements, duplicate fields, repeated
invalid contents, replacement during preparation and contextual generic validators.

The synthetic Unity Editor diagnostic uses two built-ins versus 63 additional
drawings (32,256 points; approximately 1.44 MB serialized), seven measured
iterations after warm-up and a positive-controlled current-thread allocation-event
recorder. For the larger room, changing-save medians moved from **447.953 to
246.407 ms** and **2,021,655 to 1,013,837 allocation events**. An unchanged save
moved from **476.474 to 62.993 ms** and **2,021,653 to 6,003 events**. Small changing
saves measured **12.263 to 13.385 ms**, so this is not a universal latency win.
These are desktop synchronous stage costs, not allocation bytes, Quest frame
times or permission to increase capacity. The temporary diagnostic was removed
from the owned mirror after execution.

Final verification passed **1,352 EditMode and 114 selected PlayMode tests**, with
zero failures or skips. The initial full sweep passed 1,350 and failed only the
catalog-path requirement because the local harness omitted
MAESTRO_BEHAVIOUR_CATALOG; the complete corrected sweep includes the final sixth
new regression. No assertion was relaxed. PlayMode coverage includes editor
publication, recovery, temporary rooms/memory and accepted-edit lifecycle.
Catalog definitions remain identical; the two runtime fingerprints were refreshed,
and web catalog provenance passes.

The **3,190 native inputs** are recorded. The tested mirror matches source except
four documented Configure outputs: Oculus config newline normalization, enabled
Meta AR occlusion, Android Vulkan selection and the PendingCollision layer.
Runtime and final test sources match exactly; all **14 preserved local files**
remain unchanged. The earlier full PlayMode/offline journeys and Android compile
remain at their recorded a12cf3db checkpoint; this change has no new Android or
physical acceptance claim.

Explicit persistent editing still waits for validation, serialization and durable
publication on the interaction thread. The next capacity gate is owned asynchronous
save preparation/publication with candidate revision, lifecycle and resource
ownership preserved. No successful human or agent result may precede the durable
commit, and stale work must not overwrite intervening edits. Candidate copies,
reconciliation and history also retain room-wide costs. Saved limits are unchanged.
No headset, paid provider, signing, deployment or Meta operation ran. V1 remains

## Prepared-save storage boundary and verification gap — 2026-10-17

RoomStorage now has a bounded background preparation API. It reserves one
preparation before copying caller state, captures the saved baseline before the
asynchronous boundary, and refuses busy coordinator ownership without waiting.
The worker validates and encodes private data and proves the resulting wire bytes
through the strict read path. Hash-cache inspection holds only short cache locks
during this background work; normal synchronous Save ordering remains unchanged.

A prepared candidate is private, belongs to its exact store, and supports one
publication attempt. Publication rechecks the captured primary contents and newer
formats, preserves damaged bounded originals as unreadable evidence, and refuses
an existing pending file. A stale candidate cannot overwrite an intervening save.
Disposal, cancellation and failure release preparation admission; foreign stores
cannot consume another store's candidate. Empty or oversized primaries cannot be
captured. This is a per-store count bound, not a whole-process memory guarantee.

The storage API is not yet connected to native edit completion. Existing book,
program and agent calls still follow their synchronous editor paths. Native
integration must retain the journal candidate, native resources and asset leases,
recheck current revision/lifecycle, and report pending/failure/commit accurately.
Storage fingerprints do not establish in-memory ownership. Final file staging,
flush, backup/publication and potential retention-lock waits remain synchronous.

The desktop probe records seven measurements after warm-up using the same
63-drawing/32,256-point room. Current synchronous changing-save median is
**245.713 ms**. Prepared dispatch is **4.052 ms**, remaining worker wait
**184.527 ms**, and publication **47.367 ms**. Dispatch/publication record **3,157 /
1,322 current-thread allocation events**; worker allocations are not measured.
Two-built-in medians are **13.300 ms** for ordinary changing save and
**2.113 / 0.796 / 12.471 ms** for dispatch/wait/publication. The diagnostic blocks
only to measure its isolated worker phase; production UI must await/yield.
These are synthetic Editor stage costs, not a user-visible speedup, frame-time
acceptance or justification for larger saved limits. Publication is still too
costly to treat as an invisible frame operation.

Verification initially found malformed generated .meta files: Unity ignored the
new files while 16 older tests passed. That run is not accepted as new-code
coverage. Metadata was corrected, and the log gate now rejects Unity's ignored
asset/invalid GUID diagnostic even when the editor exits zero. The new gate
regression fails against the old gate and passes after the fix. The corrected
focused run passed 37 tests, including its then-current 21 new cases.

Final verification passed **1,374 EditMode and 114 selected PlayMode tests** with
zero failures or skips, explicitly including all **22 final preparation tests**.
Coverage includes caller snapshot isolation, concurrent-save refusal, a busy disk
coordinator, preparation without a long-held publication lock, one-shot ownership,
cancellation, size/format protection and admission release. Native catalog
definitions are unchanged; two source records were added and two refreshed.

All **3,196 frozen native inputs** match the tested mirror except the same four
Configure outputs documented above (Oculus newlines, Meta AR occlusion, Vulkan and
PendingCollision). All **14 preserved local files** remain unchanged. The temporary
probe was removed. The full offline journeys and Android compile remain at their
earlier a12cf3db checkpoint. No headset, paid provider, signing, deployment or Meta
operation ran; V1 remains incomplete.

Next: integrate prepared saving with the shared native operation lifecycle and
remove the remaining publication wait without allowing stale edits, premature
success, leaked reservations or replay after interruption.

## First native pending-save integration — 2026-10-17

Object rotation now enters the bounded prepared-save path through the shared
capability module used by the original book form, agent and programs. Its catalog
duration changes from instant to completion. The operation keeps its journal and
workspace ownership until preparation drains; it reports pending, failure and
confirmed completion rather than treating queue admission as success.

Runtime time progression and personal viewpoint movement can coexist with an
unrelated prepared edit without rewinding those live values. The saved checkpoint
can be older, so these newer runtime values remain dirty after the edit completes.
Authored changes and physics placement capture still invalidate stale candidates;
explicit world-time edits retain their exact-clock requirement.

Only the target body is held while its rotation is prepared. Cancellation restores
the previous trajectory only if the motion owner and physics revision still match.
Teleport, grab, changed physics and another owner invalidate it. Disable/focus
loss/suspension cancels before publication, and detached cleanup never accesses a
destroyed Unity object. Final native preparation, conditional file publication and
journal acceptance remain a non-yielding owner-thread commit.

This migrates the shared rotation action, not every edit. Final publication still
does synchronous filesystem work; no Quest latency or large-world capacity claim
is made. Other edit operations, a publication reservation and device/provider/
release acceptance remain outstanding. Current saved limits are unchanged.

Native completion publishes its accepted revision and saved/dirty status before
notifying observers. A callback that captures newer runtime state keeps that
state dirty. The non-yielding commit also refuses recursive completion and ignores
retraction attempts once commit begins; the saved operation remains one-shot.

## Owned worker staging for pending room edits — 2026-10-17

RoomStorage's prepared-save worker now writes and durably flushes its private
candidate before returning readiness. A live handle pins the stage against ordinary
writers. The final conditional commit verifies its exact bytes and saved baseline,
claims .pending without overwriting a foreign writer, and preserves the old valid
primary as backup or the damaged primary as unreadable evidence. Prepared stages
are not accepted room documents and are never adopted on restart.

Publication refuses busy room/memory coordination or retained-file inspection
without waiting on the owner thread. The interactive staged path makes one final
replace attempt, without Windows retry sleeps. Its worker-computed candidate hash
is reused for the inspection cache after successful publication; this removes a
redundant owner-thread hash without replacing content checks with file metadata.
The shared rotation completion action remains the sole native edit integration.
Other synchronous APIs retain their existing contract.

Thirteen new regression cases cover private pinned staging, stale/newer primaries,
foreign files/directories, wrong-store ownership, cancellation/disposal, damaged
baseline evidence, abandoned and substituted stages, busy retention/coordinator
ownership, and a real Windows reader preventing replacement. Final checks pass
**1,389 EditMode**, **51 selected PlayMode** and **23 web capability tests**.
The native selection includes the existing pending-operation lifecycle, program,
physical-book, receipt and save-recovery checks. All **3,202 native inputs** match
the final mirror apart from documented Configure-derived settings; all **14**
preserved local files remain unchanged. Capability contracts and bundled guides
are unchanged; generated native provenance is refreshed.

The same synthetic desktop probe, with seven measured iterations after warmup,
records **4.988 ms dispatch**,
**267.319 ms worker wait** and
**51.051 ms publication** for 63 drawings
(1,439,584 encoded bytes), versus **266.030 ms** for a
synchronous changing save. Worker allocation events are not measured. The first
staging version still spent 56.914 ms in publication; reusing its worker-computed
cache hash removes redundant work, but remaining content checks and IO are still
too substantial to claim a frame-safe Quest commit. The probe blocks only its
diagnostic harness while measuring worker wait; production polls completion.
These are storage diagnostics, not current headset performance or capacity proof.

Remaining work includes bounded primary/stage reads and hashes, backup copying,
final replacement, native reconciliation and cleanup IO on the owner thread.
Crash/partial-write stages remain preserved evidence; bounded orphan retention
and recovery are still required before broad migration. Do not silently adopt or
delete them. A genuinely asynchronous publication boundary must define native
reservation/retirement and exact acceptance semantics before splitting the current
non-yielding commit. The other edit families still need explicit completion
operations and resource ownership. Saved limits remain unchanged.

## Bounded unfinished-save maintenance — 2026-10-17

Private prepared saves now reserve a filesystem slot under the shared room
coordinator before writing. Admission permits four preparations of up to 4 MiB
each, counting active and abandoned files across stores; unexpected oversized or
unsafe entries block further preparation. There is no automatic orphan sweep,
adoption, or overwrite. Existing room-document capacity is unchanged.

Two shared room-domain completion actions inspect and explicitly discard exact
unfinished preparations, with cached status/entry facts and the feature
roomStagingMaintenance.v1. Book catalog forms, programs and delegated requests
use these same definitions. Inspections expose at most 32 entries, opaque review
IDs, eligibility, byte counts and fingerprints, without private paths or contents.
The worker hashes pinned inactive files outside the room coordinator.

Disposal needs explicit intent and the exact reviewed identity, fingerprint and
confirmation. It freezes new edits, retains a workspace lifetime reader, rechecks
the file, claims it by rename, checks the claimed bytes again and removes only
that file. Accepted primaries, backups and unrelated recovery evidence remain
protected. Cancellation, lifecycle changes and failed attempts invalidate the
review. An interrupted operation can have completed its filesystem effect; its
status never promises rollback or authorizes automatic replay. Success cleanup
preserves an inspection for the next action, while cancellation invalidates it.

This resolves bounded retention and exact cleanup for the private preparation
family. General corrupted/oversized file recovery remains in the existing
workspace recovery workflow. It does not migrate other synchronous editing APIs
or eliminate remaining owner-thread publication and reconciliation work.

Final verification passes **1,403 EditMode**, **56 selected PlayMode** and
**1,442 web room tests in 143 files**, with zero failures or skips. The new coverage
includes 14 storage cases and five native integration journeys: exact shared
receipts/facts and non-replay, active writer/pinned-file refusal, capacity recovery
followed by a real rotation, cancellation/retirement draining, and changed-byte
preservation. Actual native receipt output is committed as the web contract fixture.

The first scene run reproduced an inspection invalidation bug during ordinary
scheduler completion cleanup (55 passed, one failed); completion now retains the
delivered review while cancellation clears it. The final rerun above includes
that regression. Initial compilation and failed-run evidence are retained.

## Owned background publication boundary — 2026-10-17

RoomStorage now has a worker publication operation for an already prepared save.
Dispatch transfers the candidate exactly once without normal-path file IO on the
caller. The existing single-preparation admission slot remains held through the
worker and cleanup. Disposing the old wrapper, a duplicate request or a different
store cannot steal the transferred candidate. The synchronous publication API
retains its existing contract and shares the same conditional publication core.

The operation has an atomic cancellation boundary. A queued request can be
cancelled; once the worker crosses that boundary it finishes one attempt.
Completion separates confirmed saving from an unconfirmed possible write.
Failure after publication may have changed saved data and never authorizes replay.
This also covers faults in observing a completed effect. Cleanup occurs on the
worker, and admission is released before its future completes.

Eleven new regression cases verify the actual worker thread and absence of its
caller synchronization context, ownership across caller disposal, bounded
admission, cancellation before/after the boundary, wrong-store and duplicate
attempts, intervening accepted saves, coordinator contention, two competing
stores, Windows readers, and injected failures before/after durable effects.
Final checks pass **1,414 EditMode**, **56 selected PlayMode** and **263 web
catalog/capability tests in four files**, with zero failures or skips.
All **3,216 native inputs** match the verified mirror; the temporary diagnostic
source was removed. All **14 protected files** are unchanged. Catalog contracts
and ten bundled guides are unchanged; native provenance is refreshed.

The matching synthetic desktop probe uses seven measured iterations after
warmup. For 63 drawings / 1,439,585 encoded bytes, synchronous prepared publication
takes **44.2052 ms** median. Worker publication dispatch takes **0.0149 ms**
(maximum **0.0212 ms**), followed by **44.6473 ms** worker wait
(maximum **46.8259 ms**). Worker allocation events are not measured. The probe
blocks only its diagnostic harness while measuring wait; the API returns a future.
These measurements establish the storage dispatch boundary, not headset frame
time or live native integration.

The shared rotation action still performs its existing non-yielding native commit.
Integration must reserve the exact journal and native resources independently of
an observation/receipt, retain workspace lifetime, and define acceptance after
cancellation or unconfirmed saving. Current WorkspaceContent retirement destroys
native objects before waiting for detached writers; its directory writer lease
is not a native resource reservation. An unchecked await in the current commit
would therefore be incorrect. Do not relax authored-revision checks to accommodate
unrelated physics without a dependency/merge contract, or turn a pending write
into synchronous success. Existing synchronous editing APIs remain unchanged.

## Native rotation with background publication — 2026-10-17

The shared object.rotation.set action now includes final disk publication in its
background operation. The worker preflights the saved baseline and exact private
stage, releases its coordinator, and waits for owner-thread acceptance. Unity
revalidates the authored journal/history, lifecycle and target, prepares native
resources, accepts once and authorizes the same conditional publisher before
native callbacks. No disk coordinator waits for Unity, and no saved completion
receipt is issued until both native application and publication are confirmed.

This deliberately has an explicit accepted-but-saving phase. Native acceptance
precedes final IO, so Stop afterwards cannot retract either the visible edit or
the detached save. The publisher rechecks the baseline: an external change after
preflight can refuse saving. That outcome leaves the accepted live edit available
for inspection and makes storage read-only before the future completes, preventing
autosave, flush or a later ordinary edit from overwriting uncertain saved data.
Pre-acceptance failures/cancellation leave both the native pose and accepted files
unchanged. This is an inspect/recover outcome, never an automatic replay.

The operation participates in the existing saveTask ordering. A native Changed
callback may synchronously flush newer accepted state because authorization is
already signaled. New clock/viewpoint captures remain dirty. Only the target's
physics is held during preparation; it resumes after application, while unrelated
physics and world time continue. The directory write lease survives Stop/disable
until worker cleanup drains, without retaining Unity objects for final IO.

Eleven new regression cases cover unlocked preflight, explicit authorization,
cancellation before acceptance, changed saved baselines, pure-writer contention,
ongoing unrelated physics/world time, Stop/disable after acceptance, synchronous
callback flushes, post-acceptance conflict preservation and cleanup with no later
observer poll. The focused run passed 1,419 EditMode and 62 selected PlayMode
tests; all 1,442 web room tests in 143 files passed. The expanded program,
temporary-room and workspace run then passed 1,273 PlayMode tests with zero
failures or skips. That wider run precedes only the final creation-error wording
correction. The final source then passed 68 targeted EditMode and 70 selected
PlayMode tests, plus 263 catalog/capability web tests. All 3,216 native inputs
match the tested mirror except the four inspected Configure-derived settings.
All 14 protected files are unchanged. Source hashes, the diagnostic-only delta,
native reports and exact catalog comparisons are retained.

Verification evidence is recorded in .quest-evidence/native-publication-20261017/.
The synchronous APIs, other completion-action migrations, full native/device
acceptance and release gates remain outstanding. No device, paid-provider,
signing, deployment or Meta operation is included in this checkpoint.

Follow-up correctness gate: ordinary RoomStorage.Save still lacks a retained
accepted-primary baseline across independent external changes. The new
post-acceptance failure latch protects this publication path, but does not prove
that a later ordinary autosave after a pre-acceptance cancellation cannot replace
externally changed data. Harden that shared path before extending completion
editing. Legitimate newer edits from the same editor and confirmed temporary
room/memory snapshot publications must advance its known baseline explicitly;
a blanket read-only latch for every stale candidate would reject valid user work.

## Accepted saved-room identity — 2026-10-17

The ordinary-save gap identified above is now addressed in the same storage
implementation. RoomStorage opts into retained primary identity, separately from
decode caching. Load establishes the accepted disk baseline. Existing rooms
require Load before first Save; a missing primary may be initialized. Saves
check before preparation and again before replacement. Confirmed ordinary and
prepared writes advance from their exact private bytes. Own newer snapshots
therefore remain valid while obsolete preparations fail without a blanket lock.

TemporaryMemorySave receives the same RoomStorage, checks the initial paired
capture against its accepted room as well as memory, and advances the identity
after confirmed paired publication or recovery. It does not bless freshly read
outside state. Ordinary staging uses CreateNew, preserves pre-existing pending
files, and cleans only complete matching bytes created by that attempt.
Observed outside changes require recovery; transient IO failures keep the
existing dirty-state retry contract. No save-format migration is introduced.

This continues the shared-world direction: actor-specific real-room collision,
authored terrain, visibility and navigation stay in their existing shared
policies. Long-term authoring requires durable state alongside responsive
interaction; persistence checks do not introduce per-tool save implementations.
Source/tests and verification receipts are in
.quest-evidence/accepted-primary-20261017/. Physical latency acceptance,
other completion-action migrations and the previously recorded release gates
remain open.

Verification passed **1,433 EditMode**, **293 relevant PlayMode** and **243 shared
catalog/capability web tests** (four files), with no failures or skips in those
final runs. Sixteen added regressions cover primary changes, first-load ownership,
cancelled preparations, own newer writes, temporary pairs, staging preservation,
autosave and unavailable primary startup. Final PlayMode source differs from the
green full EditMode run only in three PlayMode fixture files; native implementation
is identical. The final mirror compiled and tested those fixture corrections.
All 3,220 native inputs are checked against the source, with the four documented
Configure-derived differences and 12 generated SDK/material files recorded

do not establish headset latency or complete physical/provider/release acceptance.

## Shared placement completion — 2026-10-17

Position and scale now reuse rotation's prepared-save lifecycle. One
PersistedEditOperation maps native progress into the shared scheduler and
receipts, and one BeginPlacement factory owns validation, target pose holding,
write admission and pending-edit creation. There is no per-action save pipeline.
The existing synchronous RoomEditor methods retain their completion contracts.
The legacy tray helper is named HasNoDurationParameter: omitting a user-facing
seconds field does not imply an immediate native result. Its validation and
control behavior remain unchanged; execution duration comes from the catalog.

object.position.set and object.scale.set now advertise completion duration.
Their IDs, versions, schemas, physical admission and limits remain unchanged.
The shared description makes acceptance, cancellation, save conflicts and
temporary rooms explicit. Temporary accepted edits now report local Undo and
snapshot behavior instead of suggesting a pending disk publication.

The expanded scene run exposed a genuine concurrency regression: simultaneous
moves of independently loaded objects could be refused by single-save admission.
The shared operation now waits under existing scheduler capacity/time bounds
before capturing a candidate. It revalidates target revision and temporary-session
identity, and cancellation removes the waiting action without creating a delayed
effect. Loading and simulation remain independent of serialized save preparation.
Additional native regressions cover cancellation of one waiter while another
completes, and target/session changes before admission.

This is part of preserving immersion during authoring: unrelated simulation and
world time continue while saved bytes are prepared and published. The requested
placement resets motion at acceptance, then gravity resumes. A completed action
confirms its authored saved placement; it does not promise a dynamic body stayed
at that coordinate while the save completed. Native reconciliation and snapshot
capture remain on the Unity owner thread and still require Quest measurement.

## Ordered shared placement admission — 2026-10-17
A native regression reproduced a save-admission fairness defect: after the
previous save drained, a later placement could begin before an older waiter
received its next poll. Repeated program steps could therefore keep overtaking
another ready action.

Move, rotate and resize now share a room-owned queue for save admission.
Arrival order is measured when an action has acquired its native resources and
requests this admission; unrelated loading remains independent. Waiting holds
no room copy, body or write lease. The queue is bounded by the existing scheduler
concurrency limit, and the existing completion deadline still applies.
Cancellation, target invalidation and preparation refusal release the place.
Workspace disable, focus/pause suspension and destruction clear admissions;
an interrupted request cannot revive when the editor returns. Accepted writes
retain their existing drain-and-report lifecycle.

This adds no persistent schema, migration, new database or special agent-only
execution path. Manual synchronous edits retain their existing contract and
can invalidate queued work. It does not serialize all world simulation or
promise that a physical body stays at its saved pose.

Validation: **1,434 EditMode**, **389 affected PlayMode**, and **1,786 web room/Quest UI tests in 179 files** pass.
The focused scene run passes all 11 selected cases. Three new scene regressions
cover reversed polling/late arrival with durable outcomes, cancelled/stale/
rejected heads, capacity recovery and disable/re-enable. The original failure
is retained separately as reproduction evidence.

.quest-evidence/placement-order-20261017/. All 3,224 native inputs match the final
verification mirror except the four established Configure-derived changes;
known generated SDK/material files are recorded separately. Fourteen protected
local files remain unchanged. No device/provider test, Android packaging,
signing, deployment or Meta change is included. Release and physical-performance
acceptance remain open.

Keep/Discard and direct editor fixtures use the same validation rules.

## Clock and weather completion — 2026-10-17

Clock configuration, explicit seeks and weather changes now use the common
bounded persisted-edit queue and completion receipt in the book, agent and
programs. A temporal change waits for prior saving, then resolves its delta at
native acceptance and captures that exact clock/weather checkpoint. Background
preparation and conditional publication use the existing accepted-primary guards.
The save future is installed before callbacks; completion waits for native and
durable success. Later runtime movement/time remains dirty for its next save.

Configuration Undo/Redo restores settings while preserving current day/second.
Seeks preserve settings and remember the actual pre-acceptance position, even if
the requested destination initially matched the clock. Weather starts from the
current sample at acceptance. Weather Undo/Redo starts a transition toward the
previous/next target using its duration and seed. Reapplying unchanged settings
creates no temporal history or revision churn and does not restart a fade.
Whole-document snapshot restoration retains exact snapshot semantics; these
metadata-only intents cannot conceal object/resource changes.

Weather admission seals the existing liquid episode under a short flow-only
lease. Its callbacks cannot reopen flow across the transition; cancellation,
acceptance or failure releases the lease. Collected quantities keep separate
liquid Undo and events. Clock, animation and independent physics continue.
Existing liquid-episode publication remains synchronous; dependent vessel
readiness and owner-thread snapshot/native costs remain explicit follow-up work.

Receipts retain immutable acceptance-time observations despite later ticking or
human edits. Before acceptance, cancellation leaves the requested change unapplied.
After acceptance, Stop cannot cancel any part of its save. Failed saving preserves
disk and leaves the live change unconfirmed, blocks saving and requires recovery;
no success or automatic replay is reported. Temporary Keep/Discard uses the same
history, while synchronous editor entry points retain save-before-return behavior.

## Liquid content history and native continuity — 2026-10-17

changes each select matching field merge and native reconciliation behavior.

later. Authored conflicts still invalidate preparation, and explicit measured
transfers retain their existing exclusive ownership requirements. Container
configuration/removal remains a full component edit. These rules apply through
the shared simulation, manual and agent paths.

publication still saves synchronously before success, reverts on failure, and
releases its writer before accepted flow events. Moving it to workers still needs

events, lifecycle draining and unconfirmed-save recovery. Owner-thread snapshot
costs and physical timing acceptance remain open.

## Bounded asynchronous liquid checkpoints — 2026-10-17

Automatic pour, scoop and rain episodes now accept their paired liquid quantities
into the canonical journal immediately and save immutable snapshots asynchronously
in order behind earlier room saves. At most two checkpoints can be pending.
A full queue pauses additional intake without accumulating missed time or pinning
bodies. Preparing an authored edit seals existing flow and holds new intake until
the edit accepts or drains. Clock/weather and liquid saving share the accepted
checkpoint worker and unconfirmed-save handling.

The shared world.liquidPersistence fact exposes idle, saving and unconfirmed,
pending/capacity counts and an error through the same catalog used by the user,
agent and programs. The existing savedAmountMl live field means accepted journal
balance, not disk durability. Failed storage retains accepted quantities, preserves
saved files and blocks further saves until explicit workspace recovery. A later
queued save cannot overwrite a failed ancestor. Restarting physics is not recovery.

The editor owns queued completion across removal of the pouring component.
Successful amount events wait for durable storage and a safe episode boundary.
Each checkpoint releases its writer before notification; later accepted checkpoints
may still own writers. Undo or authored participant changes invalidate obsolete
notifications. Moving the virtual world during saving preserves a valid quantity
event. Listener mutations or a new episode stop the rest of a delivery batch.
Temporary forks remain local and reload never replays notifications.

## Physical sculpt, scoop and packing completion — 2026-10-18

Releasing a sculpt, scoop or packing gesture now starts the same ordered
completion/save path used by book, agent and program edits. The physical capture
owns progression; explicit retry returns a handle to that same operation.
Capture facts report saving while queued, preparing or awaiting durability.
Duplicate resolution cannot repeat a dose or create a second packed ball.
Mesh/collision and both material balances still change in one native transaction.

Before acceptance, cancellation, pause/focus loss or invalidation retains the exact
draft for explicit retry/discard. After acceptance, interruption clears the
retryable draft and lets saving drain. An accepted publication failure preserves
live state and earlier saved files, blocks further writes, and identifies a packed
ball for inspection. Completion receipts and packing lastSaved appear only after
confirmed saving; old operation handles cannot stop a later gesture.

Material scooping uses an internal content scope that preserves current root
position/rotation/scale at acceptance and Undo/Redo. It cannot create/remove
identities or edit world resources. Ordinary held-tool release records runtime
movement without invalidating the material candidate. Resizing remains authored:
it saves the new scale and invalidates an older unaccepted candidate, leaving the
material draft for explicit retry. Shape and packing retain full geometry
transactions. No saved format, public capability ID or current capacity changes.

## Shared material authoring completion — 2026-10-18

Shared material-store configuration/removal, packing and carried/surface transfer
now use the same ordered completion operation as the other world edits. Inputs
are captured before waiting and both participant revisions are checked again on
admission. Packing returns its exact object ID and measured quantity only after
saving completes; transfer results are immutable. No public IDs, formats, units,
capacity limits or separate material runtime were added.

Before acceptance, cancellation or preparation failure leaves both sides intact.
After acceptance, Stop lets persistence drain. Publication failure keeps the live
changes unconfirmed and the prior files intact, blocks further saving and requires
workspace recovery. Replaying an accepted request cannot repeat its quantity or
create another ball. One Undo restores both sides. Store/transfer acceptance and
history preserve current root placement; packing keeps a full creation transaction.

## Session-owned recording completion — 2026-10-18

Live recording finish now freezes samples and joins the shared ordered edit
queue. Physical Record/Stop, lifecycle Stop, programs and agent calls use one
session-owned save attempt. A Finish receipt completes only after native
application and publication; repeated Finish observes the same attempt.
Cancelling a receipt stops observation, without cancelling the recording save.
Explicit discard can cancel an unaccepted take. Accepted publication must settle
before its buffer can be discarded; discarding an unconfirmed applied buffer
does not undo the live animation or repair saving.

Pause/focus loss freezes capture. Unaccepted preparation is retired and the same
frozen take rejoins at the tail after interaction resumes; accepted publication
continues and keeps its immutable receipt. A disabled workshop retains an
unaccepted take. A failed preparation preserves frames for exact retry; changed
target revisions or room sessions refuse retry. A failed publication preserves
old files, leaves the live result unconfirmed and blocks saving, so it must not
be blindly replayed. A physical avatar switch now waits for recording completion
before a subsequent selection can replace the model. Temporary recordings stay
in the fork until Keep. Motion formats, canonical joint/frame/time limits,
capability IDs and input/output fields are unchanged; animation.record advertises
completion and its fact adds the saving phase.

## Versioned pose-session completion — 2026-10-18

Live pose saves now use session-owned ordered completion. Physical handle release,

session/version and accepted object revision, and completes only after native
application and publication. Cancelling observation does not cancel the session.

preview instead of dropping work. Preparation failure stops posing and retains
the newest live/requested pose for exact retry. Applied/unconfirmed publication
requires recovery rather than replay. Explicit discard cancels unaccepted
