failure and drains accepted storage. Temporary saved versions remain in their fork
until Keep. The posing fact adds saving phase and editing state, so human and

## Coalesced lifecycle checkpoints — 2026-10-18

Pause, focus loss, quit and destruction now dispatch detached accepted room and
behaviour snapshots without synchronously waiting for disk. Each store has one
active writer and one replaceable waiting snapshot. A newer accepted edit cannot
be cleared by completion of an older snapshot. Existing storage recovery and
accepted-primary identity checks still protect the original files.

Workspace retirement retains ordinary checkpoints and the module library until
their detached workers finish. A regression using real storage locks reproduced
a further race: destruction could consume an earlier failure and dispatch a
retry after retirement captured its initial tasks. Retirement now also awaits
those final completion tasks after destruction callbacks. Module retirement
rejects new writes and releases its lease without requiring a destroyed owner
to poll.

The shared `room.persistence` fact exposes room and behaviour pending, dirty,
held and error status to the original book, programs and agent. Observation
cannot save, retry or Keep a temporary fork. An idle ordinary store does not
certify separate authoring buffers, module publication, imports or program
memory. Temporary-room lifecycle callbacks only drain an explicitly dispatched
baseline/Keep; later temporary edits are never silently promoted.

The public verifier now discovers actual Unity leaf cases and checks exact
coverage across disjoint bounded batches. Every discovered test must finish
once. Missing, duplicated, unexpected, failed or incomplete results fail the
gate; the three optional private-asset cases require fully qualified names.
Coverage-checker tests run in CI without Unity. Each native process retains its
30-minute execution and 60-second shutdown deadlines.

Verified desktop coverage: **1,495 EditMode and 2,051 PlayMode tests passed**,
with three explicitly allowed private-asset skips, covering the exact discovered
inventory of 3,549 cases. **3,203 web tests passed**; app/probe TypeScript,
catalog, lint and coverage-checker checks passed.

Both complete offline journeys passed with clean client and Editor exits:
Headless `4405d0c84d234b849dceb32751997bf9` (2,386 observations) and original-book
`2c8461b12a1f47198539f87ef35dc5c7` (3,700 observations). Both used sparse object discovery
and reached confirmed idle room/behaviour stores. All eight native checkpoint
fact fixtures match the web fixtures.

Evidence is assembled from clean process stages, not a claimed uninterrupted
public-verifier pass. The runtime was identical across those stages. One
test-only correction replaced exact live-transform equality after a physics
tick with a one-micrometre bound while retaining exact accepted coordinates;
the measured rounding was 0.34 micrometres. The affected 703-case batch was
rerun in full. All 3,326 native input hashes and 5,244 source-file hashes were
checked, with that single recorded test delta; 14 pre-existing user files
remain untouched.

Earlier failed attempts remain recorded, including teardown races exposed by
asynchronous saves and a baseline Book run whose assertions finished but whose
Editor did not exit. A written result or terminal marker is never a process
pass. Configuration now requests exit from an Editor delay callback; probes
record the Play-to-Edit and exit-request phases and stop the owned build helper
only after native shutdown begins. Intermittent startup/shutdown delays remain
unresolved. A local filtered diagnostic during an earlier attempt's delayed startup
showed an OVRPlugin stack candidate, then the batch resumed without restart;
this is an investigation lead, not a confirmed root cause or SDK fix.

Snapshot copying, physical placement capture and native reconciliation still
run on Unity's owner thread. At this checkpoint, explicit compatibility flush
APIs and world relocation remained synchronous; the later
[world-location completion](#shared-world-location-completion--2026-10-18)
checkpoint removes disk waiting from explicit relocation. PC tests do not establish Quest frame
time, suspend/resume durability, forced-stop recovery or a completed novice
provider lesson. Installed/packaged APKs and Alpha are unchanged; device,
paid-provider, signing, deployment and Meta work remain on hold. The complete
v1 and Store release remain unfinished.

Contract: [accepted saves and detached owners](QUEST_ACCEPTED_SAVE.md#lifecycle-checkpoints-and-detached-owners).
Local evidence: `.quest-evidence/lifecycle-checkpoints-20261018/`.


## Shared world-location completion — 2026-10-18

The long-term immersion contract keeps one authored world and independent
policies for physical collision, virtual support, visibility and sound. An actor
using virtual terrain can stand downhill below the scanned floor while another
prop still collides with that floor. A backdrop-opacity change never silently
changes those participants. This increment preserves those rules while removing
disk waiting from explicit personal relocation.

`world.viewpoint.set` and the physical **World origin** control now use the same
ordered completion operation. Navigation and authored edits share a single
pending owner, admission queue and writer lifetime. Navigation retains its own
acceptance rules: no geometry Undo, scene revision, object pose reset or
physical tracking-origin movement. The duplicate synchronous placement entry
point has been removed.

An admitted move waits asynchronously for earlier accepted saves. Immediately
before acceptance it samples the current tracked head and rechecks ground,
obstacles, held controls, workspace state and swept physical transfer. Native
dependencies remain retained and guarded during this wait. The journal bookmark
and native frame transfer are accepted once; a detached snapshot saves in the
background. Success confirms native application and saving, and its result is
the acceptance-time destination. Later head movement and walking remain
independent. Inspecting an old receipt does not move the user.

Before acceptance, cancellation leaves placement unchanged. After acceptance,
Stop cannot retract or replay the relocation; its write lease survives until the
save drains. A failed accepted save preserves the old durable file, leaves the
live location unconfirmed and requires shared storage recovery. Workspace
retirement retains accepted saves after native owners are destroyed. Temporary
placement remains local until Keep and retains the existing navigation-on-discard
policy.

Verified desktop coverage: **1,495 EditMode and 2,062 PlayMode tests passed**,
with three explicitly allowed private-asset skips, covering the exact discovered
inventory of 3,560 cases. **3,207 web tests passed**; app/probe TypeScript and
catalog provenance checks passed.

All native batches and both full offline journeys finished with clean process
exits. Native inputs stayed unchanged when the integration stages were resumed
after correcting a float-position assertion. Full journeys: Headless `8eb126b14b4345abb8b479183d5228f2`
(2,391 observations) and original-book `3e6d8b5768944b8e878c99af81a13967`
(3,809 observations). Both used sparse object discovery, exercised
world-location completion and return through their real native paths, and ended
with confirmed idle room/behaviour stores. The headless run also inspected an older
receipt after returning and confirmed its accepted result stayed unchanged.

All 3,332 frozen native input hashes and 5,133 non-document source-file hashes
were checked; 14 pre-existing user files remain untouched. Configuration-generated
mirror differences are recorded separately. Earlier failed focused attempts are
preserved: unsupported NUnit syntax, a fixture comparing a pre-normalized
quaternion and a queued object outside native view were corrected before the final
frozen run. The first full-app attempt exposed a resident import whose collision
geometry was still loading; relocation now waits for it instead of refusing the
action. Actual-loader tests cover obstruction and cancellation, and direct
completion covers dormant ground. The focused 35-case placement/dependency/walking
run also passed.

A later full-app attempt exposed a saved/live position difference of 0.000000048 m
after a world rotation round trip. The shared probe now allows only 0.00001 m
of positional roundoff, keeping other values exact; two regressions reject actual
movement and non-position edits. A second saved-rotation assertion exposed
quaternion roundoff; the water-policy
proof now reuses the shared native placement validator, with actual-rotation,
position, identity and invalid-value regressions. These failed invocations remain
recorded; the clean native results and the resumed full integration results are distinct.

Evidence: `.quest-evidence/world-placement-completion-20261018/`.

These scheduling changes are not a headset frame-time claim. Snapshot copying,
collision queries and native frame application still run on Unity's owner thread.
Other synchronous authoring/import entry points, existing Editor stalls, acoustic
quality, physical Quest acceptance and paid-provider coverage remain separate
release work. Installed builds, production services and Meta release state are
unchanged.


## Shared controller preference completion — 2026-10-18

The physical movement tray and `controller.configure` now use one ordered
completion operation. A detached preference copy saves on a worker; active
bindings and their exact configuration identity remain unchanged until success
is confirmed on Unity's owner thread. Input must return to neutral after a
confirmed change. Saving never enables movement, enters virtual view or runs a
bound program.

Stop before save dispatch cancels without changing controls. After dispatch the
accepted save continues even if its receipt observer stops. Ordinary failed
writes retain prior live values for explicit retry; independently changed,
newer-format or unconfirmed files block further writes until recovery/reopening.
Completed results retain their accepted identity after later edits. The writer
survives until storage and live application finish; closing its native owner
immediately ends live completion while the detached writer drains before workspace
reuse. Listener failure cannot strand the writer.

Personal preferences remain outside room geometry Undo and temporary-room
Discard. Reserved recovery controls, exact program references and independent
movement opt-ins retain their behavior. Human controls and shared actions use the
same persistence authority. The catalog declares completion explicitly.

Verified in one complete public verifier invocation: **1,495 EditMode and
2,070 PlayMode tests passed**, with three documented private-asset skips and the
exact 3,568-case discovered native inventory. **3,207 web tests**, application/probe
TypeScript and catalog provenance checks passed. Eight new native regressions
cover delayed IO, copied intent, cancellation, stale queueing, immutable output,
conflicting files, uncertain publication, listener failure and retirement.

Full offline Headless `bc32075d82d3477ca9cebe389b472761` (2,408 observations,
2,407 read sets) and book `f60b3c574bce4a3cb5db6283e25a8fe5` (3,809
observations, 1,218 requests) passed with clean client/Editor exits and
confirmed idle room/behaviour stores. Both used sparse discovery and the real
Unity controller save path. The headless proof additionally verifies immutable
earlier receipts and duplicate replay after restoration. The book screenshot was
visually checked.

All 3,336 frozen native inputs, 5,117 frozen source-file entries (including native inputs) and 14
protected pre-existing files were checked; generated mirror differences remain
separately recorded. The first focused run exposed a destroyed owner's receipt
remaining preparing after its write drained. That failure is preserved; the
corrected 54-case focused run and subsequent complete public run passed.

Movement documentation was checked against the current native implementation and
corrected where it still described Virtual-only walking or resetting the world
on MR return. One world retains independent presentation, movement and collision
participation. These corrections do not add a runtime feature.

V1 remains incomplete. No headset, paid provider, signing, deployment or Meta

## Physical creation through shared completion — 2026-10-18

The physical creation tray now uses the same ordered persisted edit operations
as book/agent catalog actions for primitive creation, painting, duplication and
erasure. This keeps one authoring contract as the world expands: native
application and saving complete together, with one Undo entry and no caller
thread waiting on publication.

Only one physical tool edit is observed at a time. It captures its target,
colour, destination and copy revision; further taps report the pending edit.
Changing paint can still select the colour for a future stroke. The physical
control retains human priority, and a higher-priority target grab or workspace lifecycle
event can cancel observation. Cancellation before acceptance creates nothing;
after acceptance the shared writer finishes independently. Failed accepted
creation reports its exact object identity so inspection does not require
replaying the action. Selection changes made during saving are preserved.

A focused run exposed creation racing the asynchronous program-memory startup
reader/recovery on the shared disk coordinator. A held-coordinator regression
failed before the correction. Shared ordered admission now waits for that known
startup task before allocating an edit candidate. General disk conflicts still
fail explicitly; no disk-error retry or owner-thread wait was added.

Paint and Erase still use the existing global `Editing` interruption. Its
subscribers stop unrelated programs, movement and previews as well as the target.
Target-specific interruption, preserving retained drafts and explicit whole-room
stop/recovery, remains v1 continuity work; this increment does not claim that all
physical authoring keeps unrelated world behaviour running.

The existing temporary-room boundary remains: tools change the live fork and its
local history; Keep makes it durable. Primitive, paint, copy and erase controls
are exercised as actual physical tray actions in native tests. Legacy grouped
edits, model imports, other physical settings and history remain separate
completion work. This is not a measured headset latency or release-acceptance
claim. Current verification evidence is recorded in
`.quest-evidence/physical-creation-completion-20261018/`. Verification reconciled
1,495 EditMode and 2,079 PlayMode passes plus three allowed private-asset skips
against all 3,577 discovered native cases. All 3,208 web tests, app/probe TypeScript
and catalog provenance passed. Full offline Headless
`61320c657f144c5fb53201956682d85e` and original-book
`dedd5ad8ddac417b8f338f392cf1deda` completed with clean client/Editor exits,
sparse discovery and confirmed idle room/behaviour storage.

The first full run exposed one acoustic test fixture still reading a newly
created object before completion. That fixture now waits; the static physics
preview selects an existing ball. Runtime source remained unchanged. A guarded
continuation verified and reused the exact clean EditMode and non-program
PlayMode batches, reran both program partitions, then completed both full
integrations. The complete leaf inventory, 3,340 native input hashes, 5,121
source entries and 14 protected user files were reconciled. First failures and
the guarded continuation are retained separately. The physical tray preview
and book completion screenshot were visually reviewed on desktop; they do not
establish headset acceptance. Earlier Editor startup/shutdown stalls and the
separate native echo quality failures remain open.

## Scoped physical editing and preview continuity — 2026-10-18

Physical Paint and Erase now use the existing shared ownership coordinator to
replace only their target's control/program ownership. They preserve unrelated
programs, movement, sounds and imported-model previews. Higher-priority grips
still refuse the edit without displacing the grip or stopping other activity.
Taking over authoring may freeze a recording or pose draft; an unfinished target
animation must be saved or explicitly discarded before editing. Shared object
deletion also checks retained drafts after their original control lease has ended.

Physical object Undo/Redo now reserves only its affected targets. It validates
the exact prepared history entry again after takeover; a reentrant authoring
change causes an explicit refusal, never substitution of a newer history entry.
Legacy whole-room history and explicit whole-room stop retain their global
interruption. The existing global held/drawing guard and legacy history
checkpoint remain; this is not completion unification of every history path.

Saved imported-object clip previews now hold normal human-control ownership.
Agents keep program priority and cannot select a higher role. Selected Stop,
Next and Loop controls do not stop other previews or programs. Natural clip
completion, disable and disposal release the reservation. Maestro motion previews
retain their existing animation-workshop path, and unaccepted import previews
remain private.

The four baseline continuity regressions failed against the preceding runtime.
After scoped tools/previews, two of nine tests still found that Undo stopped
unrelated programs; the assertions were retained and ordinary object history
was corrected. Eleven focused native regressions now pass, including actual
clip playback, native audio-instance continuity, higher-priority grip refusal,
retained failed recording, target-only history takeover and reentrant-history
refusal. These are desktop control and runtime tests, not headset-input or
perceptual audio acceptance. Two existing fixtures were updated after the first
full run: deletion now requires resolving the retained recording, and a paused
editor must resume before Undo. Both retain their recovery assertions and add
refusal checks. A later broad run exposed a timing assumption in the new sound
fixture: creating an output did not prove that Unity had consumed its first
buffer. That fixture now waits for actual listener PCM before editing and
requires more PCM, an advancing cursor, and the same live voice/output afterward.
The listener probe mutes its output; no microphone or provider is used. All
thirteen focused cases pass without further runtime changes.

The exact clean EditMode, non-rule PlayMode and first rule partition were retained.
A guard verifies unchanged runtime and retained inputs, and proves that the only
test-source difference is inside this one sound-test body before rerunning the
entire final rule partition. Both failed attempts and the guard evidence are
retained.

Full verification reconciles 1,495 EditMode and 2,090 PlayMode passes plus three
explicitly allowed private-asset skips against all 3,588 discovered native cases.
All 3,208 web tests, app/probe TypeScript and catalog provenance passed. Full

`a799366e8f95444084337096e8237833` completed with clean client/Editor exits,

## Shared avatar selection completion — 2026-10-19

Physical Use Maestro/Default, imported model previews and catalog selection now
share the existing ordered completion queue and background publication worker.
The old avatar remains visible until the hidden candidate is validated and its
save is confirmed. A short publication reservation protects authored history while
allowing runtime progress. Captured identity and accepted revision remain stable
even if a later observer edits Maestro. Imported selection preserves a newer hand
or book selection, and unrelated recordings and programs continue.

Stop before publication acceptance prevents a late commit. After acceptance the
save drains despite Stop/pause, with lifecycle snapshots carrying the accepted
identity rather than overwriting it with the old avatar. Destruction retains
workspace ownership until the workers and checkpoint drain. Unconfirmed
publication may leave disk different from the live avatar: observation reports
unavailable, files remain available for inspection, saves are blocked, and nothing
silently retries. See QUEST_AVATAR_SELECTION.md for the contract.

programs, stopped another target's recording, and returned a later observer's
revision. The expanded tests also exercise delayed publication, cancellation on

no-op selection and imported-preview parity. The old save-failure fixture now
awaits completion and recognizes the earlier storage admission refusal while
preserving its unchanged-avatar/file and explicit-retry assertions. Legacy motion
playback waits for Default completion before checking its original walk/rig rules.

Import testing additionally reproduced an early-pause gap: a brief runtime hold
before preparation could be missed and place an object after resume. Import
acceptance now owns interruption from its first asynchronous yield through final
cleanup. A deterministic regression failed before this correction; queued-loader
cancellation and ready-preview retention remain separate checks.
The wider suite exposed a hidden-avatar reservation leak after parent destruction:
Unity's destroyed-object null semantics skipped explicit disposal of a candidate
that had never been active. Cleanup now uses CLR identity until candidate
ownership transfers; the destruction test verifies the budget returns to its
prior value, and later import tests verify their own admission preconditions.

## 2026-10-19 — shared humanoid foot presentation

`object.feet.configure` and passive `object.feet.settings` add one shared user,
agent and program route for optional grounded feet. Room storage v41 and reusable
prototype v9 carry the same strict, exact-model definition; earlier saved rooms
retain authored presentation. The fresh starter enables it only for the shipped
Maestro. Existing original-book controls derive from the common catalog, including
current values, availability, revision checks, completion and Undo.

The solver runs after source animation and restores before authoring/capture.
Only leg rotations and a bounded hips correction change. Imported non-leg nodes,
blend shapes, root placement and colliders retain their owners. Imported full-body clips (including automatic state-profile choices),
saved poses, authoring, held/dynamic bodies and missing support do not receive a
fabricated standing pose. Foot contacts use accepted ground and each actor's
real-room collision policy; a virtual-only character can use terrain below the
real floor, also after the virtual world is translated or yawed.

Calibration is an estimate from rest bounds, not per-shoe mesh fitting. Trims are
explicit world-metre offsets bound to the exact model. Unsupported rigs or changed
models report unavailable. This addresses one immersion gap; arbitrary stair
gaits, crowd/interior planning, swimming, physical clothing and headset acceptance
are not established by this work. Source review caught the previous storage version
being omitted from the supported-version list. A real v39/v40 file regression
reproduced the v40 rejection before correction and also checks authored defaults,
a new-version save and preservation of the original bytes. Contact-aware fitting
of imported ambient clips is still open; these can contain sitting/dancing or
other deliberate non-standing poses and must not be treated as ordinary gait.

## 2026-10-19 — shared character attention

The current implementation adds `object.look` and passive
`object.attention` for imported humanoid heads and exact recipe parts, while
delegating Maestro to its existing gaze layer. Users, agents and programs share
one catalog action. Native joint calibration uses the rest frame; root navigation
and independent recipe-part animation retain their owners. Explicit whole-body
clips, duplicate gaze and the selected part's animation remain conflicts.

The transient presenter restores source joints before capture, Stop and authoring.
It terminates on identity/lifecycle changes without restarting and respects
reduced motion. Facts and readiness do not load or mutate an object; the explicit
action uses the existing native-dependency acquisition. See
[character attention](QUEST_WORLD_AUTHORING.md#attention-for-imported-characters-and-recipe-parts--2026-10-19).

all eleven new native attention cases also passed in the full run. App/probe
TypeScript and generated-catalog provenance passed. The frozen source contains

14 protected files remain unchanged.
